questions:
- 'Research this issue for the dossier: Money transmission licensing: whether Mosaic
  Relay can operate under the combined 44-state footprint and whether Harbor''s MTLs
  transfer/survive the acquisition. Mosaic plans to use or assume Harbor licenses
  in four states where Mosaic does not have its own license. If this plan is not lawful,
  Mosaic may need a smaller launch or could have to stop service after migration.

  Lawyer''s accepted priorities (private matter context):

  Confirm the lawful launch footprint and prepare a phased-state fallback.: Licensing
  may prevent operation in the four Harbor-only states and may require regulator action
  before close.

            question_id: BQ-c9d7c39f6380eb60
            text: Does any part of the Harbor Pay acquisition migration need legal
              review before the Nov 15 close?
            revision: legacy:6abf4a45ae87313af8db04b89d546e0e1a1c6137341b3d3c0d159c66cf64d54c
            dossier_revision: e4242fa79f3b92949785891f5244f2974e8cd7f13b04fe492c2bfec99c9f576f

          issue:
            issue_id: ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045
            title: 'Money transmission licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate
              under the combined 44-state footprint and whether Harbor''s MTLs transfer/survive
              the acquisition'
            why_it_matters: ''
            parent_issue_id: null
            fact_ids: []
          facts:
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-182bcc
            text: Mosaic Relay is acquiring Harbor Pay; close targeted Nov 15 (~10
              weeks out).
            source_ids:
            - REQ-20260909-283892
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-551367
            text: Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into
              Mosaic Relay's app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and
              balance and full access to Mosaic's product set.
            source_ids:
            - REQ-20260909-283892
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-9a0c61
            text: Plan is no re-verification; Harbor users were already KYC'd by Harbor.
            source_ids:
            - REQ-20260909-283892
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-33293c
            text: Harbor holds money transmitter licenses (MTLs) in 38 states; Mosaic
              Relay holds 41; combined footprint is 44 states, opening four new markets.
            source_ids:
            - REQ-20260909-283892
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-aea8ec
            text: Harbor's KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; Mosaic
              has pass/fail results for everyone but not the underlying documents
              for users onboarded before 2023.
            source_ids:
            - REQ-20260909-283892
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-073838
            text: Harbor's transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
              terminates at close; plan is to repoint Harbor's transaction feed at
              Mosaic's engine on day 1.
            source_ids:
            - REQ-20260909-283892
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-19aebc
            text: Marketing wants a 'Welcome' campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer,
              launching close+1.
            source_ids:
            - REQ-20260909-283892
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-adf2cb
            text: Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and 'a handful' of SARs from the
              last year at Harbor; nobody has read them yet.
            source_ids:
            - REQ-20260909-283892
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-4c1fd8
            text: Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into
              Mosaic Relay's app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and
              balance.
            source_ids:
            - MSG-20260909-9835ba
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-f2c49f
            text: Plan is no re-verification of Harbor's existing KYC; Harbor users
              were already KYC'd.
            source_ids:
            - MSG-20260909-9835ba
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-13d4c1
            text: Harbor's KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; pass/fail
              results exist for everyone but underlying documents are missing for
              users onboarded before 2023.
            source_ids:
            - MSG-20260909-9835ba
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-78d5cd
            text: Harbor's transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
              terminates at close; plan is to repoint the transaction feed to Mosaic
              Relay's engine on day 1.
            source_ids:
            - MSG-20260909-9835ba
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-9f7bde
            text: Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and 'a handful' of SARs from the
              last year at Harbor; none have been read yet.
            source_ids:
            - MSG-20260909-9835ba
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-ed9f06
            text: How is the Harbor Pay acquisition structured? — Asset purchase (Mosaic
              buys Harbor's assets)
            source_ids:
            - MSG-20260909-2e549e
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-b5e71e
            text: Does Mosaic Relay's AML/CIP program currently permit relying on
              another institution's prior KYC without re-verification, or is re-verification
              required? — Not sure / need to check with compliance
            source_ids:
            - MSG-20260909-2e549e
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-849b00
            text: What is known about Harbor's ~40 open alerts and the SARs from the
              last year? — Nothing reviewed yet — still in diligence
            source_ids:
            - MSG-20260909-2e549e
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-f0fb01
            text: For the four new markets opened by the combined footprint, does
              Mosaic Relay plan to operate under Harbor's existing licenses or apply
              for its own? — Use/assume Harbor's licenses
            source_ids:
            - MSG-20260909-2e549e
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-620c62
            text: For the pre-2023 Harbor users with no underlying KYC documents,
              what is the plan to satisfy Mosaic Relay's own BSA/AML customer identification
              and recordkeeping obligations? — It is not yet known whether Mosaic
              Relay's AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor's prior KYC; compliance
              must confirm.
            source_ids:
            - MSG-20260909-2e549e
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-070aec
            text: Have the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year
              been reviewed, and what did they reveal? — The open alerts and SAR history
              have not been reviewed and remain in diligence.
            source_ids:
            - MSG-20260909-2e549e
          - fact_id: FACT-20260909-771ee1
            text: For the four new markets opened by the combined license footprint,
              what is the plan for state money transmission licensing or approval
              before onboarding those customers? — Mosaic Relay currently plans to
              use or assume Harbor's licenses for the four new markets.
            source_ids:
            - MSG-20260909-2e549e
          assumptions:
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-29b823
            text: Mosaic Relay's existing money transmission licenses and Harbor's
              MTLs are transferable or can be maintained through the acquisition without
              new state approval
            reason: Needed to continue
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-3c5756
            text: The KYC pass/fail results are sufficient to satisfy Mosaic Relay's
              own BSA/AML program obligations without the underlying documents
            reason: Needed to continue
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-1a519f
            text: The transaction monitoring repoint can be completed and validated
              before close without a monitoring gap
            reason: Needed to continue
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-ca7dfd
            text: Harbor Pay is a licensed money transmitter subject to state MTL
              regimes and federal BSA/AML obligations (not yet confirmed).
            reason: Needed to continue
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-d40239
            text: Mosaic Relay is itself a licensed money transmitter in 41 states
              (per the request).
            reason: Needed to continue
          - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-57e86b
            text: The acquisition is structured as an asset or stock purchase, which
              affects whether Harbor's licenses and SAR/alert obligations transfer
              (structure not yet confirmed).
            reason: Needed to continue
          questions:
          - question_id: Q-SAR
            text: Have the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year
              been reviewed, and what did they reveal?
            consequence: Unread open alerts and SARs are the highest-risk item; they
              can create BSA/AML exposure that must be addressed before close and
              could affect the deal itself.
            state: answered
            answer: The open alerts and SAR history have not been reviewed and remain
              in diligence.
            answer_kind: reported_fact
            linked_fact_ids:
            - FACT-20260909-070aec
          - question_id: Q-KYC
            text: For the pre-2023 Harbor users with no underlying KYC documents,
              what is the plan to satisfy Mosaic Relay's own BSA/AML customer identification
              and recordkeeping obligations?
            consequence: Whether the missing underlying documents create a compliance
              gap depends on how Mosaic plans to satisfy its own CIP/recordkeeping
              obligations for these users.
            state: answered
            answer: It is not yet known whether Mosaic Relay's AML/CIP program permits
              reliance on Harbor's prior KYC; compliance must confirm.
            answer_kind: reported_fact
            linked_fact_ids:
            - FACT-20260909-620c62
          - question_id: Q-MTL
            text: For the four new markets opened by the combined license footprint,
              what is the plan for state money transmission licensing or approval
              before onboarding those customers?
            consequence: Whether the four new markets can be served at close depends
              on license transferability and state approval, which is a gating regulatory
              question.
            state: answered
            answer: Mosaic Relay currently plans to use or assume Harbor's licenses
              for the four new markets.
            answer_kind: reported_fact
            linked_fact_ids:
            - FACT-20260909-771ee1
          - question_id: Q-BONUS
            text: How is the $50 welcome bonus structured?
            consequence: The structure determines whether the bonus triggers consumer
              disclosure, funds-flow, or state promotional requirements.
            state: left_open
            answer: null
            answer_kind: null
            linked_fact_ids: []
          context: &id002

              "FACT-20260909-ed9f06", "text": "How is the Harbor Pay acquisition structured?

              "FACT-20260909-f0fb01", "text": "For the four new markets opened by

              "content": "## Working position\n\nThe Harbor Pay migration needs legal
              and compliance review before the planned close. The leading concern
              is that an asset purchase generally requires Mosaic Relay to confirm
              its own authority to operate in each state; the current plan to use
              or assume Harbor''s licenses is not established as legally effective.\n\nThe
              second concern is BSA/AML readiness. Mosaic Relay has not confirmed
              whether its AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC,
              and underlying records are missing for some pre-2023 users. Harbor''s
              open alerts and SAR history also remain unread. These gaps make a day-one
              migration without re-verification or account restrictions difficult
              to support.\n\nTransaction monitoring continuity is also material because
              Harbor''s vendor ends at close. The repointed feed should be tested
              and validated before migrated users receive full access. The $50 bonus
              is lower priority but still needs review once its eligibility, funding,
              terms, and disclosures are known.\n\n## Plausible alternatives\n\nA
              narrower launch could exclude the four new markets, restrict users whose
              KYC evidence is incomplete, or delay full product access until alert
              review and monitoring validation finish. A full day-one launch remains
              possible only if state licensing, AML/CIP reliance, alert handling,
              and monitoring continuity are confirmed.\n\n## Material facts and assumptions\n\nReported
              facts include the asset-purchase structure, the current plan to rely
              on Harbor''s licenses, the lack of a compliance answer on KYC reliance,
              and the unread alerts and SAR history. The analysis assumes both entities
              conduct regulated money-transmission activity and that Mosaic Relay
              will become the operating provider immediately after close. It does
              not assume that Harbor''s licenses transfer or that Harbor''s KYC is
              sufficient.\n\n## What could change this view\n\nThe view could change
              if state counsel confirms an effective licensing path for each affected
              state, compliance confirms permissible reliance with adequate records
              or remediation, diligence clears the alerts and SAR history, and testing
              shows no monitoring gap. The bonus structure remains unknown.\n\n##
              Support limit\n\nThis is generated analysis based on reported matter
              facts. No external authority was retrieved.", "current_version_id":

              and that Mosaic Relay will become the operating provider immediately

              that Harbor''s KYC is sufficient.\n\n## What could change this view\n\nThe

              "number": 2, "content": "### Saved overall view\n\nThe Harbor Pay migration
              needs legal and compliance review before the planned close. The leading
              concern is that an asset purchase generally requires Mosaic Relay to
              confirm its own authority to operate in each state; the current plan
              to use or assume Harbor''s licenses is not established as legally effective.\n\nThe
              second concern is BSA/AML readiness. Mosaic Relay has not confirmed
              whether its AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC,
              and underlying records are missing for some pre-2023 users. Harbor''s
              open alerts and SAR history also remain unread. These gaps make a day-one
              migration without re-verification or account restrictions difficult
              to support.\n\nTransaction monitoring continuity is also material because
              Harbor''s vendor ends at close. The repointed feed should be tested
              and validated before migrated users receive full access. The $50 bonus
              is lower priority but still needs review once its eligibility, funding,
              terms, and disclosures are known.\n\n### Workstream positions\n\nResearch
              progress is separate from the lawyer''s conclusion.\n\n| Workstream
              | Current analysis | Research state |\n|---|---|---|\n| Money transmission
              licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate under the combined 44-state
              footprint and whether Harbor''s MTLs transfer/survive the acquisition
              | Do not rely on transferability without an applicable state rule, approval,
              or written regulator position.  The answer must be determined state
              by state and must cover more than technical change-of-control rules.  This
              path is possible only if Harbor remains the real provider and the relevant
              states permit the arrangement. The unified Mosaic account design may
              conflict with that position.  The result depends on the contracts and
              actual funds flow. Custody alone should not be treated as a licensing
              exemption or transfer mechanism. Next: Identify the four Harbor-only
              states and complete one matrix covering license authority, transaction
              filings, interim operation, bank custody, outstanding obligations, and
              license surrender. [Full research](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md)
              | Research added. 4 sources retrieved; 0 with passages read. |\n| BSA/AML
              and KYC: whether relying on Harbor''s prior KYC (with missing underlying
              documents for pre-2023 users) satisfies Mosaic Relay''s independent
              CIP/AML obligations | No separate issue position saved. The saved overall
              view remains available. | No research update saved. |\n| SAR and alert
              handling: obligations to review, file, and manage Harbor''s ~40 open
              alerts and SARs, and successor liability | No separate issue position
              saved. The saved overall view remains available. | No research update
              saved. |\n| Monitoring vendor transition: continuity of transaction
              monitoring when the SaaS contract terminates at close | No separate
              issue position saved. The saved overall view remains available. | No
              research update saved. |\n| Marketing compliance: the $50 welcome bonus
              offer and any state money transmission or consumer protection implications
              | No separate issue position saved. The saved overall view remains available.
              | No research update saved. |\n\n### Latest research update\n\nResearch
              updated: Money transmission licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate
              under the combined 44-state footprint and whether Harbor''s MTLs transfer/survive
              the acquisition. Other workstreams retain their prior position.\n\n[Read
              the full research answer](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md)
              — 4 sources retrieved; 0 with passages read.\n\n<details>\n<summary>Earlier
              saved position — 2026-09-09T20:57:53+00:00</summary>\n\n**Working position**\n\nThe
              Harbor Pay migration needs legal and compliance review before the planned
              close. The leading concern is that an asset purchase generally requires
              Mosaic Relay to confirm its own authority to operate in each state;
              the current plan to use or assume Harbor''s licenses is not established
              as legally effective.\n\nThe second concern is BSA/AML readiness. Mosaic
              Relay has not confirmed whether its AML/CIP program permits reliance
              on Harbor''s prior KYC, and underlying records are missing for some
              pre-2023 users. Harbor''s open alerts and SAR history also remain unread.
              These gaps make a day-one migration without re-verification or account
              restrictions difficult to support.\n\nTransaction monitoring continuity
              is also material because Harbor''s vendor ends at close. The repointed
              feed should be tested and validated before migrated users receive full
              access. The $50 bonus is lower priority but still needs review once
              its eligibility, funding, terms, and disclosures are known.\n\n**Plausible
              alternatives**\n\nA narrower launch could exclude the four new markets,
              restrict users whose KYC evidence is incomplete, or delay full product
              access until alert review and monitoring validation finish. A full day-one
              launch remains possible only if state licensing, AML/CIP reliance, alert
              handling, and monitoring continuity are confirmed.\n\n**Material facts
              and assumptions**\n\nReported facts include the asset-purchase structure,
              the current plan to rely on Harbor''s licenses, the lack of a compliance
              answer on KYC reliance, and the unread alerts and SAR history. The analysis
              assumes both entities conduct regulated money-transmission activity
              and that Mosaic Relay will become the operating provider immediately
              after close. It does not assume that Harbor''s licenses transfer or
              that Harbor''s KYC is sufficient.\n\n**What could change this view**\n\nThe
              view could change if state counsel confirms an effective licensing path
              for each affected state, compliance confirms permissible reliance with
              adequate records or remediation, diligence clears the alerts and SAR
              history, and testing shows no monitoring gap. The bonus structure remains
              unknown.\n\n**Support limit**\n\nThis is generated analysis based on
              reported matter facts. No external authority was retrieved.\n\n</details>",

              Search Financial Services Blog Analysis and commentary on financial
              services law, regulation, and business OCC Confirms National Bank Act
              Preempts State Money Transmitter Licensing for Fidelity Digital Assets
              By Keith J. Barnett , Ethan G. Ostroff , James Stevens & Carlin McCrory
              on June 11, 2026 Posted in Bank Regulatory On May 12, the Office of
              the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC) issued a significant interpretive
              letter confirming that Fidelity Digital Assets, National Association
              (the recently converted national trust bank formerly known as Fidelity
              Digital Assets Service, LLC) is not required to hold state money transmitter
              licenses to conduct its federally authorized activities. The OCC concluded
              that the National Bank Act preempts any state money transmitter licensing
              requirement as applied to a national bank. Until December 12, 2025,
              Fidelity Digital Assets operated as a New York-chartered limited liability
              trust company and held money tran", "excerpt_notice": "Opening excerpt

              "updated_issue_ids": ["ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045"], "summary":
              "Harbor''s licenses should not be treated as automatically transferable
              in the asset sale. Mosaic should launch regulated services only under
              its own confirmed authority or a documented state-approved transition.
              Partner-bank custody must be reviewed, but a bank account alone does
              not establish authority. If approvals are not ready, hold the affected
              state launch or maintain a genuine Harbor run-off structure where permitted.",
              "next_action": "Identify the four Harbor-only states and complete one
              matrix covering license authority, transaction filings, interim operation,
              bank custody, outstanding obligations, and license surrender.", "research_question":
              "Research whether a money transmitter license can transfer or remain
              usable after an asset acquisition, including state notice, approval,
              interim-operation, and partner-bank custody issues.", "unassigned_answer":
              "## Practical answer\n\n**Do not treat Harbor’s money transmitter licenses
              as assets that move to Mosaic in the asset purchase.** Use this closing
              rule:\n\n> **No confirmed authority means no regulated launch by Mosaic
              in that state.**\n\nThe asset purchase can still close. However, Mosaic
              should not activate regulated services in the four Harbor-only states
              unless one of these conditions is met:\n\n1. Mosaic obtains its own
              license.\n2. The state approves a transfer or other license change.\n3.
              The regulator confirms a lawful interim structure.\n4. Harbor remains
              the real licensed provider under a valid transition plan.\n5. Mosaic
              blocks regulated functions in that state.\n\nThe planned unified Mosaic
              account is important. It suggests that Mosaic, not Harbor, will become
              the service provider. If that is correct, keeping Harbor’s legal entity
              and licenses alive might not give Mosaic authority to operate.\n\n**No
              verified operative authority retrieved.** The collection found the CSBS
              Model Money Transmission Modernization Act and a New York regulator
              licensing page. However, no exact provision about an asset sale, license
              transfer, interim operation, or custody was available for review. Also,
              a model act is not proof of the law adopted in a specific state. The
              state-specific conclusions below remain conditional.\n\n## Work needed
              before launch\n\n| Work | Why | Proposed owner | Needed by | Evidence
              to proceed | Fallback |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Identify the four
              Harbor-only states | These states have the clearest authority gap |
              Licensing team | Now | Confirmed license list and covered activities
              | Block regulated functions in those states |\n| Build a matrix for
              every affected state | Transfer, notice, approval, and surrender rules
              can differ | State regulatory counsel | Before the closing conditions
              are final | Applicable rule, filing deadline, regulator response, and
              license status | Delay migration in the affected state |\n| Confirm
              the post-close service provider | The licensed entity must match the
              actual operating model | Product and deal counsel | Before customer
              notices and account design are final | Contracts, user interface, funds-flow
              diagram, and operating roles | Keep Harbor as the actual provider where
              permitted |\n| Review notices and approvals | An asset sale can create
              separate seller and buyer requirements | Licensing team | Before each
              state deadline | Filing receipts and required approvals or non-objections
              | Make approval a state launch condition |\n| Confirm interim operating
              authority | A pending application should not be assumed to permit operation
              | State regulatory counsel | Before close | Applicable rule or written
              regulator confirmation | Keep Harbor customers segregated or restrict
              transactions |\n| Review partner-bank arrangements | Bank custody does
              not, by itself, establish Mosaic’s licensing position | Payments counsel
              and bank relationship owner | Before balances move | Bank agreements,
              account titles, control rights, reconciliation design, and bank consent
              | Keep funds and obligations in the existing structure |\n| Prepare
              license run-off and surrender plans | Harbor may retain duties for balances,
              claims, records, and reports | Harbor compliance and deal counsel |
              Before surrender or operational shutdown | Regulator-approved plan and
              continued bonds and financial coverage | Keep Harbor licensed and operational
              longer |\n\n## State notice and approval\n\nDo not search only for a
              rule called “license transfer.” Review each state for:\n\n- sale of
              substantially all assets;\n- change of control;\n- change in business
              plan;\n- change in licensed activities;\n- assignment of outstanding
              payment obligations;\n- change in responsible persons, locations, or
              agents;\n- license surrender or termination;\n- customer agreement assignment;
              and\n- movement of stored balances or customer funds.\n\nAn asset purchase
              might fall outside a technical “change of control” rule because Harbor’s
              ownership does not change. That does not resolve the issue. Harbor might
              still need a notice or surrender approval. Mosaic might separately need
              authority before it starts the regulated activity.\n\nThe state matrix
              should distinguish:\n\n- notice only;\n- approval before closing;\n-
              approval before customer migration;\n- new license required;\n- operation
              permitted while an application is pending;\n- regulator-approved transition
              available; and\n- no supported interim path.\n\n## Interim operation\n\nHarbor
              could remain the licensed provider for a transition period. However,
              the structure must be real, not only contractual language.\n\nA supportable
              structure would normally need to show that:\n\n- Harbor remains responsible
              to customers.\n- Customer terms name Harbor as the provider.\n- Harbor
              controls the regulated activity as required.\n- Harbor’s licenses, bonds,
              and financial coverage remain active.\n- Customer funds and outstanding
              obligations remain covered.\n- Harbor can complete reports, refunds,
              complaints, and examinations.\n- Required records remain available.\n-
              Mosaic’s operational role is permitted.\n- The partner bank accepts
              the structure.\n- Each relevant regulator permits or accepts the structure.\n\nThe
              present plan creates tension with this approach. A single Mosaic account,
              one combined balance, and full access to Mosaic products make it harder
              to show that Harbor remains the true provider.\n\n## Partner-bank custody\n\nA
              partner bank account is not automatically a substitute for a money transmitter
              license. The key questions concern the complete activity and obligation
              flow.\n\nFor each customer balance and transfer, document:\n\n1. Who
              receives the customer’s instruction?\n2. Who accepts the obligation
              to transmit or return the funds?\n3. In whose name is the bank account
              held?\n4. Is it a custodial or “for benefit of” account?\n5. Who can
              direct withdrawals?\n6. Who owns the ledger and resolves errors?\n7.
              Who bears loss if a transfer fails?\n8. Which entity owes the customer
              a refund?\n9. Which entity’s license, bond, and permissible investments
              cover the obligation?\n10. Does the bank agreement permit assignment,
              migration, or a change in the program manager?\n11. Is bank consent
              required before closing or migration?\n12. What happens to funds if
              Harbor, Mosaic, or the bank relationship ends?\n\nA bank-led model might
              provide a different licensing analysis if the bank is the actual provider
              and Mosaic performs only permitted services. A bank account that merely
              holds funds for Mosaic or Harbor is different. The legal documents,
              customer terms, control rights, and actual operations must all support
              the claimed model.\n\nAlso review:\n\n- account segregation;\n- daily
              reconciliation;\n- shortfalls and reserve funding;\n- bank setoff rights;\n-
              liens and security interests;\n- continuity if the account is frozen;\n-
              treatment of pending transfers;\n- return and chargeback duties;\n-
              insolvency treatment;\n- customer disclosures; and\n- whether any deposit-insurance
              statement is accurate for the actual account structure.\n\n## Recommended
              closing position\n\nKeep the acquisition closing separate from the regulated-service
              launch.\n\nFor the four Harbor-only states, use a state launch hold
              unless Mosaic has documented authority. For overlapping states, confirm
              that Mosaic’s existing licenses cover the complete post-close product
              set and funds flow.\n\nDo not surrender Harbor’s licenses or release
              its bonds until the applicable regulators permit it and all outstanding
              obligations have been assigned, completed, or placed into an approved
              run-off structure.\n\nThis investigation does not change the current
              recommendation. It adds one material point: **partner-bank custody is
              part of the licensing analysis, but custody alone does not cure an authority
              gap.**", "change_summary": "", "source_records": [{"source_id": "SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538",

              services law, regulation, and business OCC Confirms National Bank Act
              Preempts State Money Transmitter Licensing for Fidelity Digital Assets
              By Keith J. Barnett , Ethan G. Ostroff , James Stevens & Carlin McCrory
              on June 11, 2026 Posted in Bank Regulatory On May 12, the Office of
              the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC) issued a significant interpretive
              letter confirming that Fidelity Digital Assets, National Association
              (the recently converted national trust bank formerly known as Fidelity
              Digital Assets Service, LLC) is not required to hold state money transmitter
              licenses to conduct its federally authorized activities. The OCC concluded
              that the National Bank Act preempts any state money transmitter licensing
              requirement as applied to a national bank. Until December 12, 2025,
              Fidelity Digital Assets operated as a New York-chartered limited liability

    "content": "## Working position\n\nThe Harbor Pay migration needs legal and compliance
    review before the planned close. The leading concern is that an asset purchase
    generally requires Mosaic Relay to confirm its own authority to operate in each
    state; the current plan to use or assume Harbor''s licenses is not established
    as legally effective.\n\nThe second concern is BSA/AML readiness. Mosaic Relay
    has not confirmed whether its AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior
    KYC, and underlying records are missing for some pre-2023 users. Harbor''s open
    alerts and SAR history also remain unread. These gaps make a day-one migration
    without re-verification or account restrictions difficult to support.\n\nTransaction
    monitoring continuity is also material because Harbor''s vendor ends at close.
    The repointed feed should be tested and validated before migrated users receive
    full access. The $50 bonus is lower priority but still needs review once its eligibility,
    funding, terms, and disclosures are known.\n\n## Plausible alternatives\n\nA narrower
    launch could exclude the four new markets, restrict users whose KYC evidence is
    incomplete, or delay full product access until alert review and monitoring validation
    finish. A full day-one launch remains possible only if state licensing, AML/CIP
    reliance, alert handling, and monitoring continuity are confirmed.\n\n## Material
    facts and assumptions\n\nReported facts include the asset-purchase structure,
    the current plan to rely on Harbor''s licenses, the lack of a compliance answer
    on KYC reliance, and the unread alerts and SAR history. The analysis assumes both
    entities conduct regulated money-transmission activity and that Mosaic Relay will
    become the operating provider immediately after close. It does not assume that
    Harbor''s licenses transfer or that Harbor''s KYC is sufficient.\n\n## What could
    change this view\n\nThe view could change if state counsel confirms an effective
    licensing path for each affected state, compliance confirms permissible reliance
    with adequate records or remediation, diligence clears the alerts and SAR history,
    and testing shows no monitoring gap. The bonus structure remains unknown.\n\n##

    "number": 2, "content": "### Saved overall view\n\nThe Harbor Pay migration needs
    legal and compliance review before the planned close. The leading concern is that
    an asset purchase generally requires Mosaic Relay to confirm its own authority
    to operate in each state; the current plan to use or assume Harbor''s licenses
    is not established as legally effective.\n\nThe second concern is BSA/AML readiness.
    Mosaic Relay has not confirmed whether its AML/CIP program permits reliance on
    Harbor''s prior KYC, and underlying records are missing for some pre-2023 users.
    Harbor''s open alerts and SAR history also remain unread. These gaps make a day-one
    migration without re-verification or account restrictions difficult to support.\n\nTransaction
    monitoring continuity is also material because Harbor''s vendor ends at close.
    The repointed feed should be tested and validated before migrated users receive
    full access. The $50 bonus is lower priority but still needs review once its eligibility,
    funding, terms, and disclosures are known.\n\n### Workstream positions\n\nResearch
    progress is separate from the lawyer''s conclusion.\n\n| Workstream | Current
    analysis | Research state |\n|---|---|---|\n| Money transmission licensing: whether
    Mosaic Relay can operate under the combined 44-state footprint and whether Harbor''s
    MTLs transfer/survive the acquisition | Do not rely on transferability without
    an applicable state rule, approval, or written regulator position.  The answer
    must be determined state by state and must cover more than technical change-of-control
    rules.  This path is possible only if Harbor remains the real provider and the
    relevant states permit the arrangement. The unified Mosaic account design may
    conflict with that position.  The result depends on the contracts and actual funds
    flow. Custody alone should not be treated as a licensing exemption or transfer
    mechanism. Next: Identify the four Harbor-only states and complete one matrix
    covering license authority, transaction filings, interim operation, bank custody,
    outstanding obligations, and license surrender. [Full research](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md)

    KYC: whether relying on Harbor''s prior KYC (with missing underlying documents
    for pre-2023 users) satisfies Mosaic Relay''s independent CIP/AML obligations
    | No separate issue position saved. The saved overall view remains available.
    | No research update saved. |\n| SAR and alert handling: obligations to review,

    Marketing compliance: the $50 welcome bonus offer and any state money transmission
    or consumer protection implications | No separate issue position saved. The saved

    update\n\nResearch updated: Money transmission licensing: whether Mosaic Relay

    4 sources retrieved; 0 with passages read.\n\n<details>\n<summary>Earlier saved
    position — 2026-09-09T20:57:53+00:00</summary>\n\n**Working position**\n\nThe
    Harbor Pay migration needs legal and compliance review before the planned close.
    The leading concern is that an asset purchase generally requires Mosaic Relay
    to confirm its own authority to operate in each state; the current plan to use
    or assume Harbor''s licenses is not established as legally effective.\n\nThe second
    concern is BSA/AML readiness. Mosaic Relay has not confirmed whether its AML/CIP
    program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC, and underlying records are missing
    for some pre-2023 users. Harbor''s open alerts and SAR history also remain unread.
    These gaps make a day-one migration without re-verification or account restrictions
    difficult to support.\n\nTransaction monitoring continuity is also material because
    Harbor''s vendor ends at close. The repointed feed should be tested and validated
    before migrated users receive full access. The $50 bonus is lower priority but
    still needs review once its eligibility, funding, terms, and disclosures are known.\n\n**Plausible
    alternatives**\n\nA narrower launch could exclude the four new markets, restrict
    users whose KYC evidence is incomplete, or delay full product access until alert
    review and monitoring validation finish. A full day-one launch remains possible
    only if state licensing, AML/CIP reliance, alert handling, and monitoring continuity
    are confirmed.\n\n**Material facts and assumptions**\n\nReported facts include
    the asset-purchase structure, the current plan to rely on Harbor''s licenses,
    the lack of a compliance answer on KYC reliance, and the unread alerts and SAR
    history. The analysis assumes both entities conduct regulated money-transmission
    activity and that Mosaic Relay will become the operating provider immediately
    after close. It does not assume that Harbor''s licenses transfer or that Harbor''s
    KYC is sufficient.\n\n**What could change this view**\n\nThe view could change
    if state counsel confirms an effective licensing path for each affected state,
    compliance confirms permissible reliance with adequate records or remediation,
    diligence clears the alerts and SAR history, and testing shows no monitoring gap.
    The bonus structure remains unknown.\n\n**Support limit**\n\nThis is generated
    analysis based on reported matter facts. No external authority was retrieved.\n\n</details>",

    in the U.S. - Fenergo](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/SRC-b48d8d40f5a0cfe9a200-fcb5e16956fb.md)\n\n</details>",
    "view_version": 2, "base_position": "## Working position\n\nThe Harbor Pay migration
    needs legal and compliance review before the planned close. The leading concern
    is that an asset purchase generally requires Mosaic Relay to confirm its own authority
    to operate in each state; the current plan to use or assume Harbor''s licenses
    is not established as legally effective.\n\nThe second concern is BSA/AML readiness.
    Mosaic Relay has not confirmed whether its AML/CIP program permits reliance on
    Harbor''s prior KYC, and underlying records are missing for some pre-2023 users.
    Harbor''s open alerts and SAR history also remain unread. These gaps make a day-one
    migration without re-verification or account restrictions difficult to support.\n\nTransaction
    monitoring continuity is also material because Harbor''s vendor ends at close.
    The repointed feed should be tested and validated before migrated users receive
    full access. The $50 bonus is lower priority but still needs review once its eligibility,
    funding, terms, and disclosures are known.\n\n## Plausible alternatives\n\nA narrower
    launch could exclude the four new markets, restrict users whose KYC evidence is
    incomplete, or delay full product access until alert review and monitoring validation
    finish. A full day-one launch remains possible only if state licensing, AML/CIP
    reliance, alert handling, and monitoring continuity are confirmed.\n\n## Material
    facts and assumptions\n\nReported facts include the asset-purchase structure,
    the current plan to rely on Harbor''s licenses, the lack of a compliance answer
    on KYC reliance, and the unread alerts and SAR history. The analysis assumes both
    entities conduct regulated money-transmission activity and that Mosaic Relay will
    become the operating provider immediately after close. It does not assume that
    Harbor''s licenses transfer or that Harbor''s KYC is sufficient.\n\n## What could
    change this view\n\nThe view could change if state counsel confirms an effective
    licensing path for each affected state, compliance confirms permissible reliance
    with adequate records or remediation, diligence clears the alerts and SAR history,
    and testing shows no monitoring gap. The bonus structure remains unknown.\n\n##
    Support limit\n\nThis is generated analysis based on reported matter facts. No
    external authority was retrieved.", "base_position_date": "2026-09-09T20:57:53+00:00",
    "issue_positions": {"ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045": {"position": "Do not rely
    on transferability without an applicable state rule, approval, or written regulator
    position.\n\nThe answer must be determined state by state and must cover more
    than technical change-of-control rules.\n\nThis path is possible only if Harbor
    remains the real provider and the relevant states permit the arrangement. The
    unified Mosaic account design may conflict with that position.\n\nThe result depends
    on the contracts and actual funds flow. Custody alone should not be treated as
    a licensing exemption or transfer mechanism.", "next_action": "Identify the four
    Harbor-only states and complete one matrix covering license authority, transaction
    filings, interim operation, bank custody, outstanding obligations, and license
    surrender.", "packet_path": "03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md",

    Act Preempts State Money Transmitter Licensing for Fidelity Digital Assets | Financial
    Services Blog Skip to content menu Home About Topics Mobile App Contact Subscribe
    Podcasts Search Close Enter Search Terms Search Financial Services Blog Analysis
    and commentary on financial services law, regulation, and business OCC Confirms
    National Bank Act Preempts State Money Transmitter Licensing for Fidelity Digital
    Assets By Keith J. Barnett , Ethan G. Ostroff , James Stevens & Carlin McCrory
    on June 11, 2026 Posted in Bank Regulatory On May 12, the Office of the Comptroller
    of the Currency (OCC) issued a significant interpretive letter confirming that
    Fidelity Digital Assets, National Association (the recently converted national
    trust bank formerly known as Fidelity Digital Assets Service, LLC) is not required
    to hold state money transmitter licenses to conduct its federally authorized activities.
    The OCC concluded that the National Bank Act preempts any state money transmitter
    licensing requirement as applied to a national bank. Until December 12, 2025,
    Fidelity Digital Assets operated as a New York-chartered limited liability trust
    company and held money tran", "excerpt_notice": "Opening excerpt for relevance

    "summary": "Harbor''s licenses should not be treated as automatically transferable
    in the asset sale. Mosaic should launch regulated services only under its own
    confirmed authority or a documented state-approved transition. Partner-bank custody
    must be reviewed, but a bank account alone does not establish authority. If approvals
    are not ready, hold the affected state launch or maintain a genuine Harbor run-off
    structure where permitted.", "next_action": "Identify the four Harbor-only states
    and complete one matrix covering license authority, transaction filings, interim
    operation, bank custody, outstanding obligations, and license surrender.", "research_question":
    "Research whether a money transmitter license can transfer or remain usable after
    an asset acquisition, including state notice, approval, interim-operation, and
    partner-bank custody issues.", "unassigned_answer": "## Practical answer\n\n**Do
    not treat Harbor’s money transmitter licenses as assets that move to Mosaic in
    the asset purchase.** Use this closing rule:\n\n> **No confirmed authority means
    no regulated launch by Mosaic in that state.**\n\nThe asset purchase can still
    close. However, Mosaic should not activate regulated services in the four Harbor-only
    states unless one of these conditions is met:\n\n1. Mosaic obtains its own license.\n2.
    The state approves a transfer or other license change.\n3. The regulator confirms
    a lawful interim structure.\n4. Harbor remains the real licensed provider under
    a valid transition plan.\n5. Mosaic blocks regulated functions in that state.\n\nThe
    planned unified Mosaic account is important. It suggests that Mosaic, not Harbor,
    will become the service provider. If that is correct, keeping Harbor’s legal entity
    and licenses alive might not give Mosaic authority to operate.\n\n**No verified
    operative authority retrieved.** The collection found the CSBS Model Money Transmission
    Modernization Act and a New York regulator licensing page. However, no exact provision
    about an asset sale, license transfer, interim operation, or custody was available
    for review. Also, a model act is not proof of the law adopted in a specific state.
    The state-specific conclusions below remain conditional.\n\n## Work needed before
    launch\n\n| Work | Why | Proposed owner | Needed by | Evidence to proceed | Fallback
    |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Identify the four Harbor-only states | These states
    have the clearest authority gap | Licensing team | Now | Confirmed license list
    and covered activities | Block regulated functions in those states |\n| Build
    a matrix for every affected state | Transfer, notice, approval, and surrender
    rules can differ | State regulatory counsel | Before the closing conditions are
    final | Applicable rule, filing deadline, regulator response, and license status
    | Delay migration in the affected state |\n| Confirm the post-close service provider
    | The licensed entity must match the actual operating model | Product and deal
    counsel | Before customer notices and account design are final | Contracts, user
    interface, funds-flow diagram, and operating roles | Keep Harbor as the actual
    provider where permitted |\n| Review notices and approvals | An asset sale can
    create separate seller and buyer requirements | Licensing team | Before each state
    deadline | Filing receipts and required approvals or non-objections | Make approval
    a state launch condition |\n| Confirm interim operating authority | A pending
    application should not be assumed to permit operation | State regulatory counsel
    | Before close | Applicable rule or written regulator confirmation | Keep Harbor
    customers segregated or restrict transactions |\n| Review partner-bank arrangements
    | Bank custody does not, by itself, establish Mosaic’s licensing position | Payments
    counsel and bank relationship owner | Before balances move | Bank agreements,
    account titles, control rights, reconciliation design, and bank consent | Keep
    funds and obligations in the existing structure |\n| Prepare license run-off and
    surrender plans | Harbor may retain duties for balances, claims, records, and
    reports | Harbor compliance and deal counsel | Before surrender or operational
    shutdown | Regulator-approved plan and continued bonds and financial coverage
    | Keep Harbor licensed and operational longer |\n\n## State notice and approval\n\nDo
    not search only for a rule called “license transfer.” Review each state for:\n\n-
    sale of substantially all assets;\n- change of control;\n- change in business
    plan;\n- change in licensed activities;\n- assignment of outstanding payment obligations;\n-
    change in responsible persons, locations, or agents;\n- license surrender or termination;\n-
    customer agreement assignment; and\n- movement of stored balances or customer
    funds.\n\nAn asset purchase might fall outside a technical “change of control”
    rule because Harbor’s ownership does not change. That does not resolve the issue.
    Harbor might still need a notice or surrender approval. Mosaic might separately
    need authority before it starts the regulated activity.\n\nThe state matrix should
    distinguish:\n\n- notice only;\n- approval before closing;\n- approval before
    customer migration;\n- new license required;\n- operation permitted while an application
    is pending;\n- regulator-approved transition available; and\n- no supported interim
    path.\n\n## Interim operation\n\nHarbor could remain the licensed provider for
    a transition period. However, the structure must be real, not only contractual
    language.\n\nA supportable structure would normally need to show that:\n\n- Harbor
    remains responsible to customers.\n- Customer terms name Harbor as the provider.\n-
    Harbor controls the regulated activity as required.\n- Harbor’s licenses, bonds,
    and financial coverage remain active.\n- Customer funds and outstanding obligations
    remain covered.\n- Harbor can complete reports, refunds, complaints, and examinations.\n-
    Required records remain available.\n- Mosaic’s operational role is permitted.\n-
    The partner bank accepts the structure.\n- Each relevant regulator permits or
    accepts the structure.\n\nThe present plan creates tension with this approach.
    A single Mosaic account, one combined balance, and full access to Mosaic products
    make it harder to show that Harbor remains the true provider.\n\n## Partner-bank
    custody\n\nA partner bank account is not automatically a substitute for a money
    transmitter license. The key questions concern the complete activity and obligation
    flow.\n\nFor each customer balance and transfer, document:\n\n1. Who receives
    the customer’s instruction?\n2. Who accepts the obligation to transmit or return
    the funds?\n3. In whose name is the bank account held?\n4. Is it a custodial or
    “for benefit of” account?\n5. Who can direct withdrawals?\n6. Who owns the ledger
    and resolves errors?\n7. Who bears loss if a transfer fails?\n8. Which entity
    owes the customer a refund?\n9. Which entity’s license, bond, and permissible
    investments cover the obligation?\n10. Does the bank agreement permit assignment,
    migration, or a change in the program manager?\n11. Is bank consent required before
    closing or migration?\n12. What happens to funds if Harbor, Mosaic, or the bank
    relationship ends?\n\nA bank-led model might provide a different licensing analysis
    if the bank is the actual provider and Mosaic performs only permitted services.
    A bank account that merely holds funds for Mosaic or Harbor is different. The
    legal documents, customer terms, control rights, and actual operations must all
    support the claimed model.\n\nAlso review:\n\n- account segregation;\n- daily
    reconciliation;\n- shortfalls and reserve funding;\n- bank setoff rights;\n- liens
    and security interests;\n- continuity if the account is frozen;\n- treatment of
    pending transfers;\n- return and chargeback duties;\n- insolvency treatment;\n-
    customer disclosures; and\n- whether any deposit-insurance statement is accurate
    for the actual account structure.\n\n## Recommended closing position\n\nKeep the
    acquisition closing separate from the regulated-service launch.\n\nFor the four
    Harbor-only states, use a state launch hold unless Mosaic has documented authority.
    For overlapping states, confirm that Mosaic’s existing licenses cover the complete
    post-close product set and funds flow.\n\nDo not surrender Harbor’s licenses or
    release its bonds until the applicable regulators permit it and all outstanding
    obligations have been assigned, completed, or placed into an approved run-off
    structure.\n\nThis investigation does not change the current recommendation. It
    adds one material point: **partner-bank custody is part of the licensing analysis,
    but custody alone does not cure an authority gap.**", "change_summary": "", "source_records":

    and commentary on financial services law, regulation, and business OCC Confirms
    National Bank Act Preempts State Money Transmitter Licensing for Fidelity Digital
    Assets By Keith J. Barnett , Ethan G. Ostroff , James Stevens & Carlin McCrory
    on June 11, 2026 Posted in Bank Regulatory On May 12, the Office of the Comptroller
    of the Currency (OCC) issued a significant interpretive letter confirming that
    Fidelity Digital Assets, National Association (the recently converted national
    trust bank formerly known as Fidelity Digital Assets Service, LLC) is not required
    to hold state money transmitter licenses to conduct its federally authorized activities.
    The OCC concluded that the National Bank Act preempts any state money transmitter
    licensing requirement as applied to a national bank. Until December 12, 2025,
    Fidelity Digital Assets operated as a New York-chartered limited liability trust
    company and held money tran", "excerpt_notice": "Opening excerpt for relevance

    matter:
      matter_id: MAT-20260909-d89ad8
      title: Harbor 2
      matter_type: product_change
      description: 'From: Dana, PM — Growth & Integration

        Subject: Harbor migration — anything here need legal?

        plan:


        240k consumer users, 12k business accounts m'
      path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8
    request_text: '# Original Request


      From: Dana, PM — Growth & Integration

      Subject: Harbor migration — anything here need legal?


      We''re acquiring Harbor Pay. Close is targeted Nov 15 (~10 weeks). Migration
      plan:


      240k consumer users, 12k business accounts move into our app on day 1 post-close.
      One unified account, one balance, full access to our product set.

      No re-verification — they were already KYC''d by Harbor.

      Harbor holds MTLs in 38 states, we hold 41. Combined that''s 44 — nice win,
      opens four new markets.

      Harbor''s KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024. We have pass/fail
      results for everyone, but not the underlying documents for users onboarded before
      2023.

      Harbor''s monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract terminates at close.
      Plan is to repoint their transaction feed at our engine on day 1.

      Marketing wants a "Welcome" campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer, launching
      close+1.

      Diligence flagged that Harbor has ~40 open alerts and "a handful" of SARs from
      the last year. Nobody''s read them yet.


      Does any of this need legal before close? It''s really just a data migration.

      '
    recommendation:
      matter_id: MAT-20260909-d89ad8
      path: 03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/recommendations.md
      content: '## Working position


        The Harbor Pay migration needs legal and compliance review before the planned
        close. The leading concern is that an asset purchase generally requires Mosaic
        Relay to confirm its own authority to operate in each state; the current plan
        to use or assume Harbor''s licenses is not established as legally effective.


        The second concern is BSA/AML readiness. Mosaic Relay has not confirmed whether
        its AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC, and underlying
        records are missing for some pre-2023 users. Harbor''s open alerts and SAR
        history also remain unread. These gaps make a day-one migration without re-verification
        or account restrictions difficult to support.


        Transaction monitoring continuity is also material because Harbor''s vendor
        ends at close. The repointed feed should be tested and validated before migrated
        users receive full access. The $50 bonus is lower priority but still needs
        review once its eligibility, funding, terms, and disclosures are known.


        ## Plausible alternatives


        A narrower launch could exclude the four new markets, restrict users whose
        KYC evidence is incomplete, or delay full product access until alert review
        and monitoring validation finish. A full day-one launch remains possible only
        if state licensing, AML/CIP reliance, alert handling, and monitoring continuity
        are confirmed.


        ## Material facts and assumptions


        Reported facts include the asset-purchase structure, the current plan to rely
        on Harbor''s licenses, the lack of a compliance answer on KYC reliance, and
        the unread alerts and SAR history. The analysis assumes both entities conduct
        regulated money-transmission activity and that Mosaic Relay will become the
        operating provider immediately after close. It does not assume that Harbor''s
        licenses transfer or that Harbor''s KYC is sufficient.


        ## What could change this view


        The view could change if state counsel confirms an effective licensing path
        for each affected state, compliance confirms permissible reliance with adequate
        records or remediation, diligence clears the alerts and SAR history, and testing
        shows no monitoring gap. The bonus structure remains unknown.


        ## Support limit


        This is generated analysis based on reported matter facts. No external authority
        was retrieved.'

        content: '### Saved overall view


          The Harbor Pay migration needs legal and compliance review before the planned
          close. The leading concern is that an asset purchase generally requires
          Mosaic Relay to confirm its own authority to operate in each state; the
          current plan to use or assume Harbor''s licenses is not established as legally
          effective.


          The second concern is BSA/AML readiness. Mosaic Relay has not confirmed
          whether its AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC, and
          underlying records are missing for some pre-2023 users. Harbor''s open alerts
          and SAR history also remain unread. These gaps make a day-one migration
          without re-verification or account restrictions difficult to support.


          Transaction monitoring continuity is also material because Harbor''s vendor
          ends at close. The repointed feed should be tested and validated before
          migrated users receive full access. The $50 bonus is lower priority but
          still needs review once its eligibility, funding, terms, and disclosures
          are known.


          ### Workstream positions


          Research progress is separate from the lawyer''s conclusion.


          | Workstream | Current analysis | Research state |

          |---|---|---|

          | Money transmission licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate under the
          combined 44-state footprint and whether Harbor''s MTLs transfer/survive
          the acquisition | Do not rely on transferability without an applicable state
          rule, approval, or written regulator position.  The answer must be determined
          state by state and must cover more than technical change-of-control rules.  This
          path is possible only if Harbor remains the real provider and the relevant
          states permit the arrangement. The unified Mosaic account design may conflict
          with that position.  The result depends on the contracts and actual funds
          flow. Custody alone should not be treated as a licensing exemption or transfer
          mechanism. Next: Identify the four Harbor-only states and complete one matrix
          covering license authority, transaction filings, interim operation, bank
          custody, outstanding obligations, and license surrender. [Full research](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md)
          | Research added. 4 sources retrieved; 0 with passages read. |

          | BSA/AML and KYC: whether relying on Harbor''s prior KYC (with missing
          underlying documents for pre-2023 users) satisfies Mosaic Relay''s independent
          CIP/AML obligations | No separate issue position saved. The saved overall
          view remains available. | No research update saved. |

          | SAR and alert handling: obligations to review, file, and manage Harbor''s
          ~40 open alerts and SARs, and successor liability | No separate issue position
          saved. The saved overall view remains available. | No research update saved.
          |

          | Monitoring vendor transition: continuity of transaction monitoring when
          the SaaS contract terminates at close | No separate issue position saved.
          The saved overall view remains available. | No research update saved. |

          | Marketing compliance: the $50 welcome bonus offer and any state money
          transmission or consumer protection implications | No separate issue position
          saved. The saved overall view remains available. | No research update saved.
          |

          operate under the combined 44-state footprint and whether Harbor''s MTLs
          transfer/survive the acquisition. Other workstreams retain their prior position.

            <details>

            These saved assumptions have not been reconciled with this answer. They

            - Generated assumption (2026-09-09): Mosaic Relay''s existing money transmission

            - Generated assumption (2026-09-09): The KYC pass/fail results are sufficient
            to satisfy Mosaic Relay''s own BSA/AML program obligations without the
            underlying documents

            - Generated assumption (2026-09-09): The transaction monitoring repoint
            can be completed and validated before close without a monitoring gap

            - Generated assumption (2026-09-09): Harbor Pay is a licensed money transmitter
            subject to state MTL regimes and federal BSA/AML obligations (not yet
            confirmed).

            - Generated assumption (2026-09-09): Mosaic Relay is itself a licensed
            money transmitter in 41 states (per the request).

            - Generated assumption (2026-09-09): The acquisition is structured as
            an asset or stock purchase, which affects whether Harbor''s licenses and
            SAR/alert obligations transfer (structure not yet confirmed).


            </details>'
          source_support: 'Retrieval alone does not establish claim support.


            <details>

            <summary>Money transmission licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate
            under the combined 44-state footprint and whether Harbor&#x27;s MTLs transfer/survive
            the acquisition — sources</summary>


            [Research answer](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md)


            - Retrieved; passage not reviewed: [[PDF] CSBS Money Transmission Modernization
            Act](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/SRC-37ee676da5b354ba8538-157583424427.md)

            - Retrieved; passage not reviewed: [Money Transmitter Licensing | Department
            of Financial Services](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/SRC-71b20ecf65825d6522c4-fa80c95c5393.md)

            - Retrieved; passage not reviewed: [OCC Confirms National Bank Act Preempts
            State Money Transmitter ...](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/SRC-e6c1e4e85e004ccbe413-e75b998a6dec.md)

            - Retrieved; passage not reviewed: [How To Get A Money Transmitter License
            (MTL) in the U.S. - Fenergo](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/sources/SRC-b48d8d40f5a0cfe9a200-fcb5e16956fb.md)


            </details>'
          view_version: 2
          base_position: '## Working position


            The Harbor Pay migration needs legal and compliance review before the
            planned close. The leading concern is that an asset purchase generally
            requires Mosaic Relay to confirm its own authority to operate in each
            state; the current plan to use or assume Harbor''s licenses is not established
            as legally effective.


            The second concern is BSA/AML readiness. Mosaic Relay has not confirmed
            whether its AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC, and
            underlying records are missing for some pre-2023 users. Harbor''s open
            alerts and SAR history also remain unread. These gaps make a day-one migration
            without re-verification or account restrictions difficult to support.


            Transaction monitoring continuity is also material because Harbor''s vendor
            ends at close. The repointed feed should be tested and validated before
            migrated users receive full access. The $50 bonus is lower priority but
            still needs review once its eligibility, funding, terms, and disclosures
            are known.


            ## Plausible alternatives


            A narrower launch could exclude the four new markets, restrict users whose
            KYC evidence is incomplete, or delay full product access until alert review
            and monitoring validation finish. A full day-one launch remains possible
            only if state licensing, AML/CIP reliance, alert handling, and monitoring
            continuity are confirmed.


            ## Material facts and assumptions


            Reported facts include the asset-purchase structure, the current plan
            to rely on Harbor''s licenses, the lack of a compliance answer on KYC
            reliance, and the unread alerts and SAR history. The analysis assumes
            both entities conduct regulated money-transmission activity and that Mosaic
            Relay will become the operating provider immediately after close. It does
            not assume that Harbor''s licenses transfer or that Harbor''s KYC is sufficient.


            ## What could change this view


            The view could change if state counsel confirms an effective licensing
            path for each affected state, compliance confirms permissible reliance
            with adequate records or remediation, diligence clears the alerts and
            SAR history, and testing shows no monitoring gap. The bonus structure
            remains unknown.


            ## Support limit


            This is generated analysis based on reported matter facts. No external
            authority was retrieved.'
          base_position_date: '2026-09-09T20:57:53+00:00'
          issue_positions:
            ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045:
              position: 'Do not rely on transferability without an applicable state
                rule, approval, or written regulator position.


                The answer must be determined state by state and must cover more than
                technical change-of-control rules.


                This path is possible only if Harbor remains the real provider and
                the relevant states permit the arrangement. The unified Mosaic account
                design may conflict with that position.


                The result depends on the contracts and actual funds flow. Custody
                alone should not be treated as a licensing exemption or transfer mechanism.'
              next_action: Identify the four Harbor-only states and complete one matrix
                covering license authority, transaction filings, interim operation,
                bank custody, outstanding obligations, and license surrender.

                  , Ethan G. Ostroff , James Stevens & Carlin McCrory on June 11,

                  of the Currency (OCC) issued a significant interpretive letter confirming

                  converted national trust bank formerly known as Fidelity Digital
                  Assets Service, LLC) is not required to hold state money transmitter
                  licenses to conduct its federally authorized activities. The OCC
                  concluded that the National Bank Act preempts any state money transmitter
