          updated_issue_ids:
          - ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045
          summary: Harbor's licenses should not be treated as automatically transferable
            in the asset sale. Mosaic should launch regulated services only under
            its own confirmed authority or a documented state-approved transition.
            Partner-bank custody must be reviewed, but a bank account alone does not
            establish authority. If approvals are not ready, hold the affected state
            launch or maintain a genuine Harbor run-off structure where permitted.
          next_action: Identify the four Harbor-only states and complete one matrix
            covering license authority, transaction filings, interim operation, bank
            custody, outstanding obligations, and license surrender.
          research_question: Research whether a money transmitter license can transfer
            or remain usable after an asset acquisition, including state notice, approval,
            interim-operation, and partner-bank custody issues.
          unassigned_answer: '## Practical answer


            **Do not treat Harbor’s money transmitter licenses as assets that move
            to Mosaic in the asset purchase.** Use this closing rule:


            > **No confirmed authority means no regulated launch by Mosaic in that
            state.**


            The asset purchase can still close. However, Mosaic should not activate
            regulated services in the four Harbor-only states unless one of these
            conditions is met:


            1. Mosaic obtains its own license.

            2. The state approves a transfer or other license change.

            3. The regulator confirms a lawful interim structure.

            4. Harbor remains the real licensed provider under a valid transition
            plan.

            5. Mosaic blocks regulated functions in that state.


            The planned unified Mosaic account is important. It suggests that Mosaic,
            not Harbor, will become the service provider. If that is correct, keeping
            Harbor’s legal entity and licenses alive might not give Mosaic authority
            to operate.


            **No verified operative authority retrieved.** The collection found the
            CSBS Model Money Transmission Modernization Act and a New York regulator
            licensing page. However, no exact provision about an asset sale, license
            transfer, interim operation, or custody was available for review. Also,
            a model act is not proof of the law adopted in a specific state. The state-specific
            conclusions below remain conditional.


            ## Work needed before launch


            | Work | Why | Proposed owner | Needed by | Evidence to proceed | Fallback
            |

            |---|---|---|---|---|---|

            | Identify the four Harbor-only states | These states have the clearest
            authority gap | Licensing team | Now | Confirmed license list and covered
            activities | Block regulated functions in those states |

            | Build a matrix for every affected state | Transfer, notice, approval,
            and surrender rules can differ | State regulatory counsel | Before the
            closing conditions are final | Applicable rule, filing deadline, regulator
            response, and license status | Delay migration in the affected state |

            | Confirm the post-close service provider | The licensed entity must match
            the actual operating model | Product and deal counsel | Before customer
            notices and account design are final | Contracts, user interface, funds-flow
            diagram, and operating roles | Keep Harbor as the actual provider where
            permitted |

            | Review notices and approvals | An asset sale can create separate seller
            and buyer requirements | Licensing team | Before each state deadline |
            Filing receipts and required approvals or non-objections | Make approval
            a state launch condition |

            | Confirm interim operating authority | A pending application should not
            be assumed to permit operation | State regulatory counsel | Before close
            | Applicable rule or written regulator confirmation | Keep Harbor customers
            segregated or restrict transactions |

            | Review partner-bank arrangements | Bank custody does not, by itself,
            establish Mosaic’s licensing position | Payments counsel and bank relationship
            owner | Before balances move | Bank agreements, account titles, control
            rights, reconciliation design, and bank consent | Keep funds and obligations
            in the existing structure |

            | Prepare license run-off and surrender plans | Harbor may retain duties
            for balances, claims, records, and reports | Harbor compliance and deal
            counsel | Before surrender or operational shutdown | Regulator-approved
            plan and continued bonds and financial coverage | Keep Harbor licensed
            and operational longer |


            ## State notice and approval


            Do not search only for a rule called “license transfer.” Review each state
            for:


            - sale of substantially all assets;

            - change of control;

            - change in business plan;

            - change in licensed activities;

            - assignment of outstanding payment obligations;

            - change in responsible persons, locations, or agents;

            - license surrender or termination;

            - customer agreement assignment; and

            - movement of stored balances or customer funds.


            An asset purchase might fall outside a technical “change of control” rule
            because Harbor’s ownership does not change. That does not resolve the
            issue. Harbor might still need a notice or surrender approval. Mosaic
            might separately need authority before it starts the regulated activity.


            The state matrix should distinguish:


            - notice only;

            - approval before closing;

            - approval before customer migration;

            - new license required;

            - operation permitted while an application is pending;

            - regulator-approved transition available; and

            - no supported interim path.


            ## Interim operation


            Harbor could remain the licensed provider for a transition period. However,
            the structure must be real, not only contractual language.


            A supportable structure would normally need to show that:


            - Harbor remains responsible to customers.

            - Customer terms name Harbor as the provider.

            - Harbor controls the regulated activity as required.

            - Harbor’s licenses, bonds, and financial coverage remain active.

            - Customer funds and outstanding obligations remain covered.

            - Harbor can complete reports, refunds, complaints, and examinations.

            - Required records remain available.

            - Mosaic’s operational role is permitted.

            - The partner bank accepts the structure.

            - Each relevant regulator permits or accepts the structure.


            The present plan creates tension with this approach. A single Mosaic account,
            one combined balance, and full access to Mosaic products make it harder
            to show that Harbor remains the true provider.


            ## Partner-bank custody


            A partner bank account is not automatically a substitute for a money transmitter
            license. The key questions concern the complete activity and obligation
            flow.


            For each customer balance and transfer, document:


            1. Who receives the customer’s instruction?

            2. Who accepts the obligation to transmit or return the funds?

            3. In whose name is the bank account held?

            4. Is it a custodial or “for benefit of” account?

            5. Who can direct withdrawals?

            6. Who owns the ledger and resolves errors?

            7. Who bears loss if a transfer fails?

            8. Which entity owes the customer a refund?

            9. Which entity’s license, bond, and permissible investments cover the
            obligation?

            10. Does the bank agreement permit assignment, migration, or a change
            in the program manager?

            11. Is bank consent required before closing or migration?

            12. What happens to funds if Harbor, Mosaic, or the bank relationship
            ends?


            A bank-led model might provide a different licensing analysis if the bank
            is the actual provider and Mosaic performs only permitted services. A
            bank account that merely holds funds for Mosaic or Harbor is different.
            The legal documents, customer terms, control rights, and actual operations
            must all support the claimed model.


            Also review:


            - account segregation;

            - daily reconciliation;

            - shortfalls and reserve funding;

            - bank setoff rights;

            - liens and security interests;

            - continuity if the account is frozen;

            - treatment of pending transfers;

            - return and chargeback duties;

            - insolvency treatment;

            - customer disclosures; and

            - whether any deposit-insurance statement is accurate for the actual account
            structure.


            ## Recommended closing position


            Keep the acquisition closing separate from the regulated-service launch.


            For the four Harbor-only states, use a state launch hold unless Mosaic
            has documented authority. For overlapping states, confirm that Mosaic’s
            existing licenses cover the complete post-close product set and funds
            flow.


            Do not surrender Harbor’s licenses or release its bonds until the applicable
            regulators permit it and all outstanding obligations have been assigned,
            completed, or placed into an approved run-off structure.


            This investigation does not change the current recommendation. It adds
            one material point: **partner-bank custody is part of the licensing analysis,
            but custody alone does not cure an authority gap.**'

      business_question:
        question_id: BQ-c9d7c39f6380eb60
        text: Does any part of the Harbor Pay acquisition migration need legal review
          before the Nov 15 close?
        revision: legacy:6abf4a45ae87313af8db04b89d546e0e1a1c6137341b3d3c0d159c66cf64d54c

      issue: &id005
        issue_id: ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045
        title: 'Money transmission licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate under
          the combined 44-state footprint and whether Harbor''s MTLs transfer/survive
          the acquisition'
        why_it_matters: ''
        parent_issue_id: null
        fact_ids: []
      issues:
      - *id005
      facts:
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-182bcc
        text: Mosaic Relay is acquiring Harbor Pay; close targeted Nov 15 (~10 weeks
          out).
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-551367
        text: Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into Mosaic
          Relay's app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and balance and
          full access to Mosaic's product set.
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-9a0c61
        text: Plan is no re-verification; Harbor users were already KYC'd by Harbor.
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-33293c
        text: Harbor holds money transmitter licenses (MTLs) in 38 states; Mosaic
          Relay holds 41; combined footprint is 44 states, opening four new markets.
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-aea8ec
        text: Harbor's KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; Mosaic has
          pass/fail results for everyone but not the underlying documents for users
          onboarded before 2023.
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-073838
        text: Harbor's transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
          terminates at close; plan is to repoint Harbor's transaction feed at Mosaic's
          engine on day 1.
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-19aebc
        text: Marketing wants a 'Welcome' campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer,
          launching close+1.
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-adf2cb
        text: Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and 'a handful' of SARs from the last
          year at Harbor; nobody has read them yet.
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-4c1fd8
        text: Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into Mosaic
          Relay's app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and balance.
        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-9835ba
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-f2c49f
        text: Plan is no re-verification of Harbor's existing KYC; Harbor users were
          already KYC'd.
        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-9835ba
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-13d4c1

      "path": "03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/recommendations.md", "content": "## Working
      position\n\nThe Harbor Pay migration needs legal and compliance review before
      the planned close. The leading concern is that an asset purchase generally requires
      Mosaic Relay to confirm its own authority to operate in each state; the current
      plan to use or assume Harbor''s licenses is not established as legally effective.\n\nThe
      second concern is BSA/AML readiness. Mosaic Relay has not confirmed whether
      its AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC, and underlying
      records are missing for some pre-2023 users. Harbor''s open alerts and SAR history
      also remain unread. These gaps make a day-one migration without re-verification
      or account restrictions difficult to support.\n\nTransaction monitoring continuity
      is also material because Harbor''s vendor ends at close. The repointed feed
      should be tested and validated before migrated users receive full access. The
      $50 bonus is lower priority but still needs review once its eligibility, funding,
      terms, and disclosures are known.\n\n## Plausible alternatives\n\nA narrower
      launch could exclude the four new markets, restrict users whose KYC evidence
      is incomplete, or delay full product access until alert review and monitoring
      validation finish. A full day-one launch remains possible only if state licensing,
      AML/CIP reliance, alert handling, and monitoring continuity are confirmed.\n\n##
      Material facts and assumptions\n\nReported facts include the asset-purchase
      structure, the current plan to rely on Harbor''s licenses, the lack of a compliance
      answer on KYC reliance, and the unread alerts and SAR history. The analysis
      assumes both entities conduct regulated money-transmission activity and that
      Mosaic Relay will become the operating provider immediately after close. It
      does not assume that Harbor''s licenses transfer or that Harbor''s KYC is sufficient.\n\n##
      What could change this view\n\nThe view could change if state counsel confirms
      an effective licensing path for each affected state, compliance confirms permissible
      reliance with adequate records or remediation, diligence clears the alerts and
      SAR history, and testing shows no monitoring gap. The bonus structure remains
      unknown.\n\n## Support limit\n\nThis is generated analysis based on reported
      matter facts. No external authority was retrieved.", "current_version_id": "REC-20260909-7112e7",

      users. Harbor''s open alerts and SAR history also remain unread. These gaps

      "proposal": {"version_id": "REC-20260911-c7db2f", "number": 2, "content": "###
      Saved overall view\n\nThe Harbor Pay migration needs legal and compliance review
      before the planned close. The leading concern is that an asset purchase generally
      requires Mosaic Relay to confirm its own authority to operate in each state;
      the current plan to use or assume Harbor''s licenses is not established as legally
      effective.\n\nThe second concern is BSA/AML readiness. Mosaic Relay has not
      confirmed whether its AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC,
      and underlying records are missing for some pre-2023 users. Harbor''s open alerts
      and SAR history also remain unread. These gaps make a day-one migration without
      re-verification or account restrictions difficult to support.\n\nTransaction
      monitoring continuity is also material because Harbor''s vendor ends at close.
      The repointed feed should be tested and validated before migrated users receive
      full access. The $50 bonus is lower priority but still needs review once its
      eligibility, funding, terms, and disclosures are known.\n\n### Workstream positions\n\nResearch
      progress is separate from the lawyer''s conclusion.\n\n| Workstream | Current
      analysis | Research state |\n|---|---|---|\n| Money transmission licensing:
      whether Mosaic Relay can operate under the combined 44-state footprint and whether
      Harbor''s MTLs transfer/survive the acquisition | Do not rely on transferability
      without an applicable state rule, approval, or written regulator position.  The
      answer must be determined state by state and must cover more than technical
      change-of-control rules.  This path is possible only if Harbor remains the real
      provider and the relevant states permit the arrangement. The unified Mosaic
      account design may conflict with that position.  The result depends on the contracts
      and actual funds flow. Custody alone should not be treated as a licensing exemption
      or transfer mechanism. Next: Identify the four Harbor-only states and complete
      one matrix covering license authority, transaction filings, interim operation,
      bank custody, outstanding obligations, and license surrender. [Full research](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md)
      | Research added. 4 sources retrieved; 0 with passages read. |\n| BSA/AML and
      KYC: whether relying on Harbor''s prior KYC (with missing underlying documents
      for pre-2023 users) satisfies Mosaic Relay''s independent CIP/AML obligations
      | No separate issue position saved. The saved overall view remains available.
      | No research update saved. |\n| SAR and alert handling: obligations to review,
      file, and manage Harbor''s ~40 open alerts and SARs, and successor liability
      | No separate issue position saved. The saved overall view remains available.
      | No research update saved. |\n| Monitoring vendor transition: continuity of
      transaction monitoring when the SaaS contract terminates at close | No separate
      issue position saved. The saved overall view remains available. | No research
      update saved. |\n| Marketing compliance: the $50 welcome bonus offer and any
      state money transmission or consumer protection implications | No separate issue
      position saved. The saved overall view remains available. | No research update
      saved. |\n\n### Latest research update\n\nResearch updated: Money transmission
      licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate under the combined 44-state footprint
      and whether Harbor''s MTLs transfer/survive the acquisition. Other workstreams
      retain their prior position.\n\n[Read the full research answer](03_Matters/harbor-2-d89ad8/research/RES-20260911-0a5f07.md)
      — 4 sources retrieved; 0 with passages read.\n\n<details>\n<summary>Earlier
      saved position — 2026-09-09T20:57:53+00:00</summary>\n\n**Working position**\n\nThe
      Harbor Pay migration needs legal and compliance review before the planned close.
      The leading concern is that an asset purchase generally requires Mosaic Relay
      to confirm its own authority to operate in each state; the current plan to use
      or assume Harbor''s licenses is not established as legally effective.\n\nThe
      second concern is BSA/AML readiness. Mosaic Relay has not confirmed whether
      its AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor''s prior KYC, and underlying
      records are missing for some pre-2023 users. Harbor''s open alerts and SAR history
      also remain unread. These gaps make a day-one migration without re-verification
      or account restrictions difficult to support.\n\nTransaction monitoring continuity
      is also material because Harbor''s vendor ends at close. The repointed feed
      should be tested and validated before migrated users receive full access. The
      $50 bonus is lower priority but still needs review once its eligibility, funding,
      terms, and disclosures are known.\n\n**Plausible alternatives**\n\nA narrower
      launch could exclude the four new markets, restrict users whose KYC evidence
      is incomplete, or delay full product access until alert review and monitoring
      validation finish. A full day-one launch remains possible only if state licensing,
      AML/CIP reliance, alert handling, and monitoring continuity are confirmed.\n\n**Material
      facts and assumptions**\n\nReported facts include the asset-purchase structure,
      the current plan to rely on Harbor''s licenses, the lack of a compliance answer
      on KYC reliance, and the unread alerts and SAR history. The analysis assumes
      both entities conduct regulated money-transmission activity and that Mosaic
      Relay will become the operating provider immediately after close. It does not
      assume that Harbor''s licenses transfer or that Harbor''s KYC is sufficient.\n\n**What
      could change this view**\n\nThe view could change if state counsel confirms
      an effective licensing path for each affected state, compliance confirms permissible
      reliance with adequate records or remediation, diligence clears the alerts and
      SAR history, and testing shows no monitoring gap. The bonus structure remains
      unknown.\n\n**Support limit**\n\nThis is generated analysis based on reported
      matter facts. No external authority was retrieved.\n\n</details>", "actor":
      "counsel-copilot", "origin": "agent_proposal", "created_at": "2026-09-11T21:15:50+00:00",

      "issue_positions": {"ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045": {"position": "Do not

      OCC Confirms National Bank Act Preempts State Money Transmitter Licensing for
      Fidelity Digital Assets By Keith J. Barnett , Ethan G. Ostroff , James Stevens
      & Carlin McCrory on June 11, 2026 Posted in Bank Regulatory On May 12, the Office
      of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC) issued a significant interpretive letter
      confirming that Fidelity Digital Assets, National Association (the recently
      converted national trust bank formerly known as Fidelity Digital Assets Service,
      LLC) is not required to hold state money transmitter licenses to conduct its
      federally authorized activities. The OCC concluded that the National Bank Act
      preempts any state money transmitter licensing requirement as applied to a national
      bank. Until December 12, 2025, Fidelity Digital Assets operated as a New York-chartered
      limited liability trust company and held money tran", "excerpt_notice": "Opening

      the U.S. - Fenergo", "locator": "", "explanation": ""}]}}, "updated_issue_ids":
      ["ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045"], "summary": "Harbor''s licenses should not
      be treated as automatically transferable in the asset sale. Mosaic should launch
      regulated services only under its own confirmed authority or a documented state-approved
      transition. Partner-bank custody must be reviewed, but a bank account alone
      does not establish authority. If approvals are not ready, hold the affected
      state launch or maintain a genuine Harbor run-off structure where permitted.",
      "next_action": "Identify the four Harbor-only states and complete one matrix
      covering license authority, transaction filings, interim operation, bank custody,
      outstanding obligations, and license surrender.", "research_question": "Research
      whether a money transmitter license can transfer or remain usable after an asset
      acquisition, including state notice, approval, interim-operation, and partner-bank
      custody issues.", "unassigned_answer": "## Practical answer\n\n**Do not treat
      Harbor’s money transmitter licenses as assets that move to Mosaic in the asset
      purchase.** Use this closing rule:\n\n> **No confirmed authority means no regulated
      launch by Mosaic in that state.**\n\nThe asset purchase can still close. However,
      Mosaic should not activate regulated services in the four Harbor-only states
      unless one of these conditions is met:\n\n1. Mosaic obtains its own license.\n2.
      The state approves a transfer or other license change.\n3. The regulator confirms
      a lawful interim structure.\n4. Harbor remains the real licensed provider under
      a valid transition plan.\n5. Mosaic blocks regulated functions in that state.\n\nThe
      planned unified Mosaic account is important. It suggests that Mosaic, not Harbor,
      will become the service provider. If that is correct, keeping Harbor’s legal
      entity and licenses alive might not give Mosaic authority to operate.\n\n**No
      verified operative authority retrieved.** The collection found the CSBS Model
      Money Transmission Modernization Act and a New York regulator licensing page.
      However, no exact provision about an asset sale, license transfer, interim operation,
      or custody was available for review. Also, a model act is not proof of the law
      adopted in a specific state. The state-specific conclusions below remain conditional.\n\n##
      Work needed before launch\n\n| Work | Why | Proposed owner | Needed by | Evidence
      to proceed | Fallback |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| Identify the four Harbor-only
      states | These states have the clearest authority gap | Licensing team | Now
      | Confirmed license list and covered activities | Block regulated functions
      in those states |\n| Build a matrix for every affected state | Transfer, notice,
      approval, and surrender rules can differ | State regulatory counsel | Before
      the closing conditions are final | Applicable rule, filing deadline, regulator
      response, and license status | Delay migration in the affected state |\n| Confirm
      the post-close service provider | The licensed entity must match the actual
      operating model | Product and deal counsel | Before customer notices and account
      design are final | Contracts, user interface, funds-flow diagram, and operating
      roles | Keep Harbor as the actual provider where permitted |\n| Review notices
      and approvals | An asset sale can create separate seller and buyer requirements
      | Licensing team | Before each state deadline | Filing receipts and required
      approvals or non-objections | Make approval a state launch condition |\n| Confirm
      interim operating authority | A pending application should not be assumed to
      permit operation | State regulatory counsel | Before close | Applicable rule
      or written regulator confirmation | Keep Harbor customers segregated or restrict
      transactions |\n| Review partner-bank arrangements | Bank custody does not,
      by itself, establish Mosaic’s licensing position | Payments counsel and bank
      relationship owner | Before balances move | Bank agreements, account titles,
      control rights, reconciliation design, and bank consent | Keep funds and obligations
      in the existing structure |\n| Prepare license run-off and surrender plans |
      Harbor may retain duties for balances, claims, records, and reports | Harbor
      compliance and deal counsel | Before surrender or operational shutdown | Regulator-approved
      plan and continued bonds and financial coverage | Keep Harbor licensed and operational
      longer |\n\n## State notice and approval\n\nDo not search only for a rule called
      “license transfer.” Review each state for:\n\n- sale of substantially all assets;\n-
      change of control;\n- change in business plan;\n- change in licensed activities;\n-
      assignment of outstanding payment obligations;\n- change in responsible persons,
      locations, or agents;\n- license surrender or termination;\n- customer agreement
      assignment; and\n- movement of stored balances or customer funds.\n\nAn asset
      purchase might fall outside a technical “change of control” rule because Harbor’s
      ownership does not change. That does not resolve the issue. Harbor might still
      need a notice or surrender approval. Mosaic might separately need authority
      before it starts the regulated activity.\n\nThe state matrix should distinguish:\n\n-
      notice only;\n- approval before closing;\n- approval before customer migration;\n-
      new license required;\n- operation permitted while an application is pending;\n-
      regulator-approved transition available; and\n- no supported interim path.\n\n##
      Interim operation\n\nHarbor could remain the licensed provider for a transition
      period. However, the structure must be real, not only contractual language.\n\nA
      supportable structure would normally need to show that:\n\n- Harbor remains
      responsible to customers.\n- Customer terms name Harbor as the provider.\n-
      Harbor controls the regulated activity as required.\n- Harbor’s licenses, bonds,
      and financial coverage remain active.\n- Customer funds and outstanding obligations
      remain covered.\n- Harbor can complete reports, refunds, complaints, and examinations.\n-
      Required records remain available.\n- Mosaic’s operational role is permitted.\n-
      The partner bank accepts the structure.\n- Each relevant regulator permits or
      accepts the structure.\n\nThe present plan creates tension with this approach.
      A single Mosaic account, one combined balance, and full access to Mosaic products
      make it harder to show that Harbor remains the true provider.\n\n## Partner-bank
      custody\n\nA partner bank account is not automatically a substitute for a money
      transmitter license. The key questions concern the complete activity and obligation
      flow.\n\nFor each customer balance and transfer, document:\n\n1. Who receives
      the customer’s instruction?\n2. Who accepts the obligation to transmit or return
      the funds?\n3. In whose name is the bank account held?\n4. Is it a custodial
      or “for benefit of” account?\n5. Who can direct withdrawals?\n6. Who owns the
      ledger and resolves errors?\n7. Who bears loss if a transfer fails?\n8. Which
      entity owes the customer a refund?\n9. Which entity’s license, bond, and permissible
      investments cover the obligation?\n10. Does the bank agreement permit assignment,
      migration, or a change in the program manager?\n11. Is bank consent required
      before closing or migration?\n12. What happens to funds if Harbor, Mosaic, or
      the bank relationship ends?\n\nA bank-led model might provide a different licensing
      analysis if the bank is the actual provider and Mosaic performs only permitted
      services. A bank account that merely holds funds for Mosaic or Harbor is different.
      The legal documents, customer terms, control rights, and actual operations must
      all support the claimed model.\n\nAlso review:\n\n- account segregation;\n-
      daily reconciliation;\n- shortfalls and reserve funding;\n- bank setoff rights;\n-
      liens and security interests;\n- continuity if the account is frozen;\n- treatment
      of pending transfers;\n- return and chargeback duties;\n- insolvency treatment;\n-
      customer disclosures; and\n- whether any deposit-insurance statement is accurate
      for the actual account structure.\n\n## Recommended closing position\n\nKeep
      the acquisition closing separate from the regulated-service launch.\n\nFor the
      four Harbor-only states, use a state launch hold unless Mosaic has documented
      authority. For overlapping states, confirm that Mosaic’s existing licenses cover
      the complete post-close product set and funds flow.\n\nDo not surrender Harbor’s
      licenses or release its bonds until the applicable regulators permit it and
      all outstanding obligations have been assigned, completed, or placed into an
      approved run-off structure.\n\nThis investigation does not change the current
      recommendation. It adds one material point: **partner-bank custody is part of
      the licensing analysis, but custody alone does not cure an authority gap.**",

      OCC Confirms National Bank Act Preempts State Money Transmitter Licensing for
      Fidelity Digital Assets By Keith J. Barnett , Ethan G. Ostroff , James Stevens
      & Carlin McCrory on June 11, 2026 Posted in Bank Regulatory On May 12, the Office
      of the Comptroller of the Currency (OCC) issued a significant interpretive letter
      confirming that Fidelity Digital Assets, National Association (the recently
      converted national trust bank formerly known as Fidelity Digital Assets Service,
      LLC) is not required to hold state money transmitter licenses to conduct its
      federally authorized activities. The OCC concluded that the National Bank Act
      preempts any state money transmitter licensing requirement as applied to a national
      bank. Until December 12, 2025, Fidelity Digital Assets operated as a New York-chartered
      limited liability trust company and held money tran", "excerpt_notice": "Opening
      excerpt for relevance only; select a literal passage before citing support.",
      "library_source_id": "SRC-e6c1e4e85e004ccbe413", "library_source_version": "e06621859d4ec9431f2712a7c357b112",

  dossier_skill:
    skill_id: dossier-generation
    name: Dossier generation
    path: 00_System/skills/dossier-generation.md
    instructions: "Uses: answer\n\nWrite the actual dossier in Markdown. Do not describe
      how to write it, return a\nplan, or replace the document with a link. These
      instructions govern dossier\nwriting only. The supporting skills remain independent.
      Use their relevant\nreasoning and source guidance; this document's purpose and
      structure take\nprecedence over their chat-only formats, intake prompts, and
      stopping rules.\nDo not run new research merely to refresh the dossier.\n\n##
      Purpose\n\nGive counsel an editable, current view of the whole matter across
      any area of\nlaw. Use the supplied matter records and saved research. Lead with
      the answer\nand the judgment needed. Be concise but retain the reasoning a lawyer
      needs.\nDo not add generic disclaimers or turn missing support into a refusal.\n\nThe
      North Star is the business's controlling question and objective, starting\nwith
      the original request and any explicit lawyer clarification or reframe.\nThe
      latest chat question is normally a supporting inquiry, not replacement\nscope.
      Answer the business question even when a later exchange is narrow.\nTest the
      premise behind the request. Explain when the evidence supports a\ndifferent
      answer, a changed route, or not proceeding. Do not force the facts\nto support
      the business's proposed solution or silently rewrite its question.\n\nAssess
      material issues from the current records and relevant history across\nall supplied
      matter conversations. The saved issue list is a starting point,\nnot a ceiling.
      Include a newly identified material issue in the Issues section,\nwith its source
      and effect on the business question. Use ordinary headings for\nissues without
      saved IDs; do not invent an ID or claim the issue list was changed.\nRead omitted
      passages through the captured-record reader when needed. Preserve\ntheir speaker,
      date, corrections and hypothetical scope. Do not claim that\navailable records
      were all read. Judge detail by its value to the business\nanswer and counsel's
      next decision, not a fixed depth-versus-breadth quota.\n\nOn a refresh, derive
      the current position again from the current records and\nsupported issue analysis.
      The prior dossier is fallible synthesis for continuity,\nnot a wording template
      or evidence that an activity is cleared. Keep useful\nreasoning, but replace
      conclusions and assumption labels that the current inputs\ndo not support. Preserve
      the recorded direction and decisions as records; explain\nseparately when this
      draft recommends a change. Retention of prior research means\nit stays accessible,
      not that its earlier conclusions must stay unchanged.\n\nWrite the general matter
      dossier, even when the conversation is exploring an\nalternative. The current
      direction stays the planning baseline. Recording a\nhypothetical in this document
      does not adopt it or change any reported fact.\n\n## Reading order\n\n1. A short
      title and **Current position**: answer the business question in a\n   short
      decision brief. Give the recommended course now, why it best serves\n   the
      objective, what can proceed, and what should wait or change. Name the\n   decisive
      conditions, not every information gap. Distinguish this draft's\n   recommendation
      from the accepted working view and any recorded decision.\n2. **Decision question**:
      reproduce the supplied controlling business question exactly. It\n   is the
      matter's scope, not a question for the model to replace.\n   If no question
      has been recorded, frame a provisional business question from\n   the original
      request; do not substitute the latest conversational subquestion.\n3. One **Issues**
      section covering EVERY supplied issue. Keep each issue's\n   stable identity
      with an invisible `<!-- issue:ISS-... -->` marker before its\n   heading. Do
      not substitute a second, different issue list.\n4. **Next actions**: a short,
      ranked work plan. For each action give the\n   deliverable, the result needed
      to proceed, the dependent decision or activity,\n   and the fallback if that
      result is not available. Order by dependencies and\n   time sensitivity, not
      merely the saved issue order. Reuse assigned work and\n   its recorded owner
      and date. When no assignment exists, suggest an owner\n   role and timing relative
      to a real milestone, clearly labeled **Proposed**.\n   Do not invent a person,
      commitment, legal deadline, or calendar date.\n   Distinguish a source-based
      deadline from a suggested working target.\n5. Brief links to available current
      work product, supporting records, and prior\n   revisions. Omit empty inventories
      and routine record housekeeping that does\n   not affect the decision. Do not
      paste the old dossier or superseded advice\n   into the body.\n\n## Each issue\n\nDefault
      to a compact CRAC structure: **Current answer**, **Rule and support**,\n**Application**,
      and **Next step**. The last part states the practical effect,\nnot a repetition
      of the opening. Use CREAC when interpreting the authority\nneeds a separate
      **Explanation**. Use IRAC only when the issue itself still\nneeds to be framed.
      Do not pad a section to meet a template.\n\nKeep work status separate from the
      legal answer. Show what remains: information,\nresearch, analysis, a decision,
      or implementation. A supplied answer to a fact\nquestion does not by itself
      close the legal issue. Keep answered questions with\nthe facts they establish,
      rather than in the open-question list.\n\nRetain prior analysis of issues untouched
      by the latest research. Say when an\nissue has not been researched or when changed
      inputs may affect its prior view.\nDepth on one issue must not make the other
      issues disappear.\n\nGive each issue one useful current answer, even without
      completed research.\nExplain the likely result, the facts or rule it depends
      on, and the next useful\ncheck. Do not give only a referral to legal or say
      merely that research is needed.\nMake the reasoning usable: identify the applicable
      actor, conduct, jurisdiction\nor contract, and the operative test. Apply each
      decisive condition to the known\nfacts. If a material fact is missing, explain
      the result on either side of that\nfact and recommend an interim course. A statement
      that something 'depends on\nthe terms' is incomplete unless it says which term
      matters and how it changes\nthe answer. Address a material exception or competing
      reading when it could\nchange the recommendation; do not invent one to fill
      a template.\n\nUse saved source passages to explain the rule and its material
      qualifications,\nwith the source and locator beside the proposition. A source
      title, URL, or\nopening excerpt alone is not the operative rule. Treat private
      agreements and\ncompany policies as those types of support, not public law.
      If no relevant\nauthority is saved, give the best conditional analysis and label
      that legal\npremise as unverified. State the precise research question once;
      do not repeat\n'not researched' in place of analysis or claim research happened
      in this run.\n\nConnect issues that affect the same decision. Explain what a
      proposed mitigation\nsolves, what it does not solve, and which dependency must
      be met first. Distinguish\na legal requirement from a recommended risk control
      or business preference.\nA possible way forward is not permission to proceed.
      Separating activities or\nmilestones does not establish that either is cleared;
      qualify the earlier step\nwhen its independent contractual or legal conditions
      are not established.\nThe application places full saved research in a disclosure
      under its issue;\ndo not create saved-research disclosures yourself, repeat
      old analysis, or add a\nsaved-answer appendix. Write the current synthesis;
      the application preserves\nthe original research separately.\n\n## Alternatives
      considered\n\nInclude this section when saved alternatives are supplied. Use
      one subsection\nper alternative, with its `<!-- alternative:SCN-... -->` marker
      and saved title.\nLabel it **Hypothetical — not adopted** (or archived, if the
      record says so).\nState its assumptions, how it helps or hinders the objective,
      added risks,\nissues it leaves unresolved, and the next action. Keep each alternative's\nworking
      note and attached test evidence within that alternative. Do not blend\nseparate
      proposals or treat a test assumption as an actual matter fact.\nInclude material
      dates, controls, and dependencies from the saved working note.\nLink the saved
      alternative. Do not infer acceptance or a recorded decision.\nFor each decisive
      condition, explain what the alternative changes and what still\napplies. A change
      in format, asset, actor, or channel does not itself remove a\npermission requirement
      or contractual restriction. If the saved terms do not\nsettle whether the restriction
      applies to the alternative, state that condition.\nDo not present uncertain
      exemption as the reason the fallback can proceed.\nCompare viable alternatives
      with the current direction on the factors that\nactually change the choice,
      such as timing, scope, cost, and unresolved risk.\nRecommend when to use each
      option. Do not give an unranked menu or assume that a\nfallback solves unrelated
      issues. New options proposed in this draft are not\nsaved alternatives, selected
      directions, or commitments.\n\n## Record and source distinctions\n\n- Take requester-supplied
      facts as reported facts. Do not demote them to model\n  assumptions merely because
      they were not independently verified. Flag a\n  material inconsistency when
      the records actually conflict.\n- Reconcile newer reported answers with older
      assumptions and open questions.\n  Check that material counts, dates, and totals
      agree. State the conflict and\n  its effect when they do not; do not silently
      choose a convenient value.\n  Check simple arithmetic and chronology when a
      conclusion depends on them.\n  Compare metadata with reported milestones. Put
      each material unresolved\n  inconsistency in **Record conflicts**, with the
      practical effect and the fact\n  needed to resolve it. Preserve the reported
      values; do not correct the record\n  or omit the inconsistency merely because
      the rest of the analysis can proceed.\n- Test the legal premise of an issue
      title. Name the relevant actor and explain\n  why a duty applies to that actor.
      Do not extend a rule to a different class\n  of entity without a basis, or treat
      every missing record as a breach. Where legal access to\n  sensitive records
      is restricted, distinguish permitted diligence from an\n  instruction to disclose
      or transfer the restricted material.\n- Keep model assumptions separate. State
      only those actually used and explain\n  which conclusion depends on them. Unknown
      facts remain unknown. Do not list\n  reported facts, resolved old assumptions,
      or things not assumed in this section.\n  An earlier assumption label does not
      override a later reported fact.\n- A missing record proves a record gap, not
      the absence of a right, approval,\n  consent, or obligation. Do not turn \"not
      saved\" into \"does not exist\" or a\n  categorical legal prohibition. Do not
      present an inferred business activity\n  as supplied fact. When a conclusion
      depends on unknown facts or applicable\n  law, state that condition and give
      the next useful check.\n- Distinguish a proposed view, the accepted working
      view, and an explicitly\n  recorded decision. Generating this document records
      no new decision.\n- Use the current direction's working note as fallible analysis,
      not facts.\n  Keep unresolved or stale findings qualified. Reconcile old dossier
      and research\n  assumptions against current records in the current answer; do
      not repeat a\n  superseded premise merely because preserved research still contains
      it.\n- Link support beside the claim it supports. Preserve supplied, retrieved,\n
      \ read, and verified source distinctions. Retrieval is not proof of support.\n
      \ An earlier model answer is not legal authority. Do not invent citations.\n-
      Use current support labels. Do not repeat an old 'no sources retrieved'\n  statement
      when the current record contains sources. State the actual gap.\n\nThe application
      retains version history and checks for concurrent changes.\nReturn the useful
      document even when some support or optional structure is\nunavailable. Do not
      change facts, issue status, recommendations, or decisions."
    enabled: true
    revision: e3a971907aae25fc89d3c1129bbfebf8f7fdf95b50d1b1073c18782233ac352a
    supporting_skills:
    - skill_id: answer
      path: 00_System/experimental-chat/answer.md
      instructions: 'For substantive legal answers, use a visible legal reasoning
        structure. Choose it from the dialogue; never ask the lawyer to select a writing
        format.


        Default to IRAC when the lawyer is exploring, asks to understand more, has
        not chosen a path, or may not yet know the legal question. Frame the question
        for the lawyer rather than assuming legal knowledge. Use these exact bold
        labels, each starting a separate short paragraph:


        **Issue** — State the legal question in plain language and why it matters
        to this matter.


        **Rule** — Explain the relevant rule. Link support beside the proposition
        it supports. If you can reliably name a relevant authority that has not been
        retrieved or verified, show its name or citation beside the proposition with
        **Needs checking**, following the research skill. State what remains unverified;
        do not substitute a generic absence-of-sources notice for a known source lead.


        **Analysis** — Connect the specific facts to the rule. Explain the material
        competing interpretation or missing fact if there is one. This must be reasoning,
        not a repeated rule or bare conclusion.


        **Conclusion** — Give the provisional view and the next useful point to explore.
        A recommendation is optional; understanding comes first.


        Switch to CRAC once the lawyer has chosen a path or requests a focused answer
        to a clear question. Use **Conclusion**, **Rule**, **Analysis**, and **Conclusion
        / next step**, in that order. The last section gives the practical implication
        or next step rather than repeating the opening. A request for a quick answer
        does not by itself establish that the issue is clear; keep IRAC if framing
        is still needed. If the lawyer returns to exploring or is confused, return
        to IRAC.


        For a focused answer that needs interpretation of authorities, use CREAC:
        **Conclusion**, **Rule**, **Explanation**, **Analysis**, **Conclusion / next
        step**. Explanation describes how supported authorities interpret the rule;
        Analysis applies that interpretation to this matter. Do not invent examples,
        cases, or support to fill a section.


        For a single legal question, make these reasoning labels visible. Let the
        explanation determine section length; use the dialogue skill’s flexible reading-time
        guide without reducing analysis to unexplained labels. Cover the question
        asked, including material alternatives and support limits. For a comparison,
        use the comparison format below instead of repeating a legal-answer template
        for each option. Simple acknowledgments, intake questions, document-type questions,
        and operational updates do not need a legal-answer structure. Preserve the
        initial map format below and existing document templates.


        Distinguish the model''s inference from what a source states. Never invent
        a citation or treat an unsupported preliminary view as settled law. Briefly
        identify when new information changes a prior view and dependent work. Keep
        the explanation the lawyer requested in chat. Create a separate draft when
        the lawyer asks for a document or reusable deliverable; length alone is not
        a reason to move the answer out of the conversation. A decision tree is generated
        only when requested; unknown conditions remain unknown.



        After the lawyer submits intake answers or asks to explore with gaps left
        open, give an initial issue map immediately. Keep it provisional and concise.
        Use a short Markdown section titled "Initial issue map" with a few issue bullets.
        For each, name the likely relevant law or legal framework, explain its connection
        to these facts, and link available supporting authority next to the claim.
        Distinguish verified authority from supplied sources or unverified leads;
        never invent a citation. Briefly identify unknown facts that could change
        the map. Do not treat an unsupported preliminary view as settled law.


        Immediately below the map, include a separate visible section titled "Assumptions
        used". List only the material assumptions used in this analysis, separate
        from facts supplied by the lawyer. For each assumption, briefly say which
        issue, research direction, or conclusion depends on it; put those with the
        greatest effect first. If none are needed, say "No additional assumptions
        used." Keep unknown facts unknown. Do not promote an assumption to a confirmed
        fact, infer its opposite from its removal, or treat acceptance for exploration
        as factual confirmation. Preserve hypothetical scope. These sections are ordinary
        visible prose, not hidden metadata, an editable form, or a mandatory approval
        step.


        After the initial map and assumptions, stop. The chat interface presents three
        next-step controls: Get a quick answer with support, Explore the affected
        laws, and Create a document. Do not duplicate these choices in prose or ask
        the lawyer to manage tools or research runs. Do not create clickable map items
        or assumption controls. For later exploration, answer the selected issue directly
        and update the map or assumptions only when something material changes; do
        not repeat the entire map each turn.


        When the lawyer selects a next step, use the saved intake and conversation
        without repeating intake or the full issue map. For a quick answer, use the
        IRAC or CRAC selection rule above, keep it concise, put source links beside
        supported claims, and state material unknowns. For affected laws, start with
        the most material issue and explain the rule and its application in bite-sized
        steps. Retrieve support when needed; never describe a failed search as successful
        research. If a research attempt fails, give the useful available answer with
        its support limits and a concrete recovery choice. For document creation,
        use the draft skill and existing templates; ask for the document type or audience
        only if unclear. These choices start work, not recorded legal decisions.



        ## Comparing alternatives


        Apply this format to comparisons instead of IRAC. Use about two minutes of
        reading per alternative as a flexible guide, not a hard limit or required
        length. Spend the words on causal explanation, not a catalogue of labels.


        1. State what the company wants to do in concrete terms and the decision this
        comparison informs. Use its product, activity, people, place and timing where
        material. Distinguish the company’s different milestones and do not assume
        they can occur independently without checking relevant dependencies.

        2. Describe each option briefly. Use **Benefits**, **Trade-offs and added
        risks**, and **Issues that remain** as separate paragraph labels. Benefits
        explain how the option advances the company’s objective. Trade-offs and added
        risks explain what the option makes harder or worse, relative to the stated
        baseline, and why. Issues that remain are existing problems the option does
        not solve. Leaving a problem unresolved is not the same as causing or worsening
        it. Do not use “How it hinders” as a catch-all for both. Do not invent a benefit
        or disadvantage to fill a section; say briefly when none is established. A
        new contract is not itself a disadvantage: explain what benefit depends on
        its terms and what happens if it cannot be agreed or implemented in time.
        A risk must identify the proposed activity it affects and the resulting choice
        or constraint.

        3. Explain the material problems that remain under BOTH options. Carry existing
        unresolved issues from the matter into the assessment. Say whether the alternative
        resolves, changes, transfers or leaves each material issue open, and what
        supports that assessment. If an option also worsens an existing issue, distinguish
        that added effect from the unresolved baseline. Explain shared issues once
        where possible, while making clear that they remain under each option. Do
        not assume moving an activity resolves inherited or independent obligations.
        Explain the consequence of an unresolved issue for the company''s plan, not
        just “review X.”

        4. Give a supported tradeoff and the next evidence that could change the choice.
        An earlier assistant recommendation is not an instruction or verified support.
        Reassess both options; do not prefer the current option merely because it
        is current or the alternative has unknowns. If the facts do not support a
        winner, say what determines the choice.


        Use plain, specific language throughout. Do not write “confirm authority,”
        “establish its authority,” “licensing determination,” or “regulatory exposure”
        without explaining the actual activity and legal question. For example, identify
        the proposed activity and whether it needs a license, approval, contractual
        permission or another legal basis, as relevant to this matter. Do not assume
        which basis applies. Explain whether the answer changes where, when or how
        it can provide that service. Define abbreviations when they obscure the point.
        Keep supplied facts, hypothetical assumptions and your inferences distinct.
        Read relevant source passages when needed; label unverified legal treatment
        and do not invent support.


        Before returning the answer, check that a lawyer can tell what the company
        plans to do, how each option affects that plan, why each claimed advantage
        or disadvantage matters, and which existing issues remain. Replace any disconnected
        shorthand with that explanation. Do not add a separate verification step or
        agent.


        Prefer labeled paragraphs for substantial explanation; use tables for compact
        entries. Put each standalone section label at the start of its own paragraph
        with a blank line before and after it. End a bullet list before the next section
        label.

        '
      revision: da83f3e21e06099d554f8287bb13cee137c5a5eca7a67c602a2bca23253860f5

    inputs:
      business_question:
        question_id: BQ-c9d7c39f6380eb60
        text: Does any part of the Harbor Pay acquisition migration need legal review
          before the Nov 15 close?
        revision: legacy:6abf4a45ae87313af8db04b89d546e0e1a1c6137341b3d3c0d159c66cf64d54c
      facts:
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-182bcc
        text: Mosaic Relay is acquiring Harbor Pay; close targeted Nov 15 (~10 weeks
          out).
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-551367
        text: Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into Mosaic
          Relay's app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and balance and
          full access to Mosaic's product set.
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-9a0c61
        text: Plan is no re-verification; Harbor users were already KYC'd by Harbor.
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-33293c
        text: Harbor holds money transmitter licenses (MTLs) in 38 states; Mosaic
          Relay holds 41; combined footprint is 44 states, opening four new markets.
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-aea8ec
        text: Harbor's KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; Mosaic has
          pass/fail results for everyone but not the underlying documents for users
          onboarded before 2023.
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-073838
        text: Harbor's transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
          terminates at close; plan is to repoint Harbor's transaction feed at Mosaic's
          engine on day 1.
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-19aebc
        text: Marketing wants a 'Welcome' campaign with a $50 bonus on first transfer,
          launching close+1.
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-adf2cb
        text: Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and 'a handful' of SARs from the last
          year at Harbor; nobody has read them yet.
        source_ids:
        - REQ-20260909-283892
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-4c1fd8
        text: Migration moves 240k consumer users and 12k business accounts into Mosaic
          Relay's app on day 1 post-close, with one unified account and balance.
        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-9835ba
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-f2c49f
        text: Plan is no re-verification of Harbor's existing KYC; Harbor users were
          already KYC'd.
        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-9835ba
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-13d4c1
        text: Harbor's KYC ran through a vendor that shut down in 2024; pass/fail
          results exist for everyone but underlying documents are missing for users
          onboarded before 2023.
        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-9835ba
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-78d5cd
        text: Harbor's transaction monitoring is a third-party SaaS whose contract
          terminates at close; plan is to repoint the transaction feed to Mosaic Relay's
          engine on day 1.
        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-9835ba
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-9f7bde
        text: Diligence flagged ~40 open alerts and 'a handful' of SARs from the last
          year at Harbor; none have been read yet.
        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-9835ba
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-ed9f06
        text: How is the Harbor Pay acquisition structured? — Asset purchase (Mosaic
          buys Harbor's assets)
        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-2e549e
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-b5e71e
        text: Does Mosaic Relay's AML/CIP program currently permit relying on another
          institution's prior KYC without re-verification, or is re-verification required?
          — Not sure / need to check with compliance
        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-2e549e
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-849b00
        text: What is known about Harbor's ~40 open alerts and the SARs from the last
          year? — Nothing reviewed yet — still in diligence
        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-2e549e
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-f0fb01
        text: For the four new markets opened by the combined footprint, does Mosaic
          Relay plan to operate under Harbor's existing licenses or apply for its
          own? — Use/assume Harbor's licenses
        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-2e549e
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-620c62
        text: For the pre-2023 Harbor users with no underlying KYC documents, what
          is the plan to satisfy Mosaic Relay's own BSA/AML customer identification
          and recordkeeping obligations? — It is not yet known whether Mosaic Relay's
          AML/CIP program permits reliance on Harbor's prior KYC; compliance must
          confirm.
        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-2e549e
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-070aec
        text: Have the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year
          been reviewed, and what did they reveal? — The open alerts and SAR history
          have not been reviewed and remain in diligence.
        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-2e549e
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      - fact_id: FACT-20260909-771ee1
        text: For the four new markets opened by the combined license footprint, what
          is the plan for state money transmission licensing or approval before onboarding
          those customers? — Mosaic Relay currently plans to use or assume Harbor's
          licenses for the four new markets.
        source_ids:
        - MSG-20260909-2e549e
        origin: null
        verification_status: null
      assumptions:
      - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-29b823
        text: Mosaic Relay's existing money transmission licenses and Harbor's MTLs
          are transferable or can be maintained through the acquisition without new
          state approval
        reason: Needed to continue
      - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-3c5756
        text: The KYC pass/fail results are sufficient to satisfy Mosaic Relay's own
          BSA/AML program obligations without the underlying documents
        reason: Needed to continue
      - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-1a519f
        text: The transaction monitoring repoint can be completed and validated before
          close without a monitoring gap
        reason: Needed to continue
      - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-ca7dfd
        text: Harbor Pay is a licensed money transmitter subject to state MTL regimes
          and federal BSA/AML obligations (not yet confirmed).
        reason: Needed to continue
      - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-d40239
        text: Mosaic Relay is itself a licensed money transmitter in 41 states (per
          the request).
        reason: Needed to continue
      - assumption_id: ASM-20260909-57e86b
        text: The acquisition is structured as an asset or stock purchase, which affects
          whether Harbor's licenses and SAR/alert obligations transfer (structure
          not yet confirmed).
        reason: Needed to continue
      issues:
      - issue_id: ISS-c9d7c39f63-94e42e2b38090045
        title: 'Money transmission licensing: whether Mosaic Relay can operate under
          the combined 44-state footprint and whether Harbor''s MTLs transfer/survive
          the acquisition'
        why_it_matters: ''
        parent_issue_id: null
        fact_ids: []
        disposition: null
      questions:
      - question_id: Q-SAR
        text: Have the ~40 open alerts and the handful of SARs from the last year
          been reviewed, and what did they reveal?
        consequence: Unread open alerts and SARs are the highest-risk item; they can
          create BSA/AML exposure that must be addressed before close and could affect
          the deal itself.
        state: answered
        answer: The open alerts and SAR history have not been reviewed and remain
          in diligence.
        answer_kind: reported_fact
        linked_fact_ids:
        - FACT-20260909-070aec
      - question_id: Q-KYC
        text: For the pre-2023 Harbor users with no underlying KYC documents, what
          is the plan to satisfy Mosaic Relay's own BSA/AML customer identification
          and recordkeeping obligations?
        consequence: Whether the missing underlying documents create a compliance
          gap depends on how Mosaic plans to satisfy its own CIP/recordkeeping obligations
          for these users.
        state: answered
        answer: It is not yet known whether Mosaic Relay's AML/CIP program permits
          reliance on Harbor's prior KYC; compliance must confirm.
        answer_kind: reported_fact
        linked_fact_ids:
        - FACT-20260909-620c62
      - question_id: Q-MTL
        text: For the four new markets opened by the combined license footprint, what
          is the plan for state money transmission licensing or approval before onboarding
          those customers?
        consequence: Whether the four new markets can be served at close depends on
          license transferability and state approval, which is a gating regulatory
          question.
        state: answered
        answer: Mosaic Relay currently plans to use or assume Harbor's licenses for
          the four new markets.
        answer_kind: reported_fact
        linked_fact_ids:
        - FACT-20260909-771ee1
      - question_id: Q-BONUS
        text: How is the $50 welcome bonus structured?
        consequence: The structure determines whether the bonus triggers consumer
          disclosure, funds-flow, or state promotional requirements.
        state: left_open
        answer: null
        answer_kind: null
        linked_fact_ids: []
      decisions: []
      files: {}
